`code/src/reward_relative/decode.py` | PROCESSING | Paper decoder model for circular variables; confirms the reference decoding was done on neural activity against position-like targets. |\n    47→\n    48→### Notes\n    49→- The reference repo is for **2-photon calcium imaging**, not electrophysiology. There is no spike sorting or ephys quality filtering in this code path.\n    50→- `sess` is the base aligned session object. The README explicitly states raw `sess` objects **do not contain dF/F**; dF/F is computed later.\n    51→- `make_session_pkl.md` shows the first saved object is `sess`, created from raw suite2p + VR files after manual suite2p curation.\n    52→- Cell curation is manual in the suite2p GUI; `iscell.npy` is the curated cell mask used when loading suite2p outputs.\n    53→- `multi_anim_sess` is the main post-processing stage used by the paper:\n    54→  - loads saved `sess` pickle for each animal/day,\n    55→  - computes dF/F,\n    56→  - optionally deconvolves dF/F into `events`,\n    57→  - adds position-binned trial matrices,\n    58→  - computes place-cell statistics and trial subset metadata.\n    59→- For single-channel data, `multi_anim_sess(..., calc_spks=True)` calls `preprocessing.dff(...)` with:\n    60→  - `neuropil_method='subtract'`\n    61→  - `baseline_method='maximin'`\n    62→  - `subtract_baseline=True`\n    63→  - `neu_coef=0.7`\n    64→  - `deconvolve=True`\n    65→  - optional `keep_teleports` per animal\n    66→- `dff` restricts valid fluorescence to trial spans using `trial_start_inds` and `teleport_inds`. Baseline is computed **within each trial** after smoothing, min-filtering, and max-filtering. The resulting dF/F is smoothed again, then OASIS is applied to get `events`.\n    67→- `append_session_data` adds behavioral timeseries aligned to imaging frames. The pipeline is therefore based on **frame-aligned** neural and behavior streams.\n    68→- `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` is the clearest reference for paper-style decoder inputs:\n    69→  - neural data are `sess.timeseries['events']`\n    70→  - trial boundaries use `trial_start_inds` and `teleport_inds`\n    71→  - reward-relative position is computed per sample from the reward-zone start for that trial\n    72→  - low-speed samples are masked out with `use_speed_thr=2`\n    73→  - lick sensor artifacts are suppressed when cumulative licks are implausibly high\n    74→  - samples with NaNs in neural data or masked speed/lick channels are dropped\n    75→- Reward-zone labels come from `behavior.get_reward_zones`:\n    76→  - `A` corresponds to zone `X` = `[80, 130]`\n    77→  - `B` corresponds to zone `Y` = `[200, 250]`\n    78→  - `C` corresponds to zone `Z` = `[320, 370]`\n    79→  - environment names remain `Env1`, `Env2`, `Env3`\n    80→- The Fig. 3 decoder notebook uses:\n    81→  - experiment `MetaLearn`\n    82→  - experiment days `[3, 5, 7, 8, 10, 12, 14]`\n    83→  - `ts_key='dff'` when loading saved `multiDayData`, but continuous neural decoder inputs are assembled from `sess.timeseries['events']` via `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`\n    84→  - occupancy matching by reward-relative position bin for some paper analyses\n    85→- Conclusion for conversion:\n    86→  - **Yes, dF/F must be computed** to match the reference processing.\n    87→  - The decoder-relevant neural stream is most likely the **deconvolved `events`**, not raw fluorescence.\n    88→  - Trial alignment should use `trial_start_inds` as the start and `teleport_inds` as the end of each trial.\n    89→\n    90→---\n    91→\n    92→## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n    93→**Status**: COMPLETE\n (n = 11 mice)” and “fixed-condition cohort (n = 3 mice)” |\n   170→| Sessions / subject | `14` imaging days for task; `m11` starts imaging on day 3 | “total of 14 days” and “for m11, imaging started on day 3” |\n   171→| Trials (total) | `12,376` imaged trials across 11 switch mice (for licking QC reference) | “81 out of 12,376 trials removed across 11 switch mice” |\n   172→| Trials / session | target `80–100`; mean `80.5 ± 7.4` across all imaging days | “We targeted 80–100 trials per session… mean ± s.d., 80.5 ± 7.4 trials” |\n   173→| Neural data time bin | imaging sampled at `~15.5 Hz` per plane | “sampled at ~15.5 Hz” / “~15.5 Hz per plane” |\n   174→| Behavior data time bin | behavior aligned to imaging frame rate `~15.5 Hz` | “All behavioral and neural time series were sampled at ~15.5 Hz” |\n   175→| Reward rate | `~85% rewarded`, `~15% omitted` | “Reward was randomly omitted on approximately 15% of trials” |\n   176→| Reward switch timing | switch after `30` trials | “Each switch occurred after 30 trials” |\n   177→| Reward zones | A=`80–130 cm`, B=`200–250 cm`, C=`320–370 cm` | “zone A, 80–130 cm; zone B, 200–250 cm; zone C, 320–370 cm” |\n   178→| Track length | `450 cm` | “450 cm linear track” |\n   179→| Decoder analysis set | `77 sessions`, `11 mice`, `7` switch days | “n = 77 sessions, 11 mice, seven switch days” |\n   180→| Decoder general result | before-switch: RR/TR/non-RR above shuffle; after-switch: only RR above shuffle | “when we tested on trials after the reward switch, only the RR population decoded… better than shuffle” |\n   181→| Decoder spatial extent | z-scored decode `>2` from `−104.5 ± 20.1 cm` to `+152.7 ± 22.9 cm` relative to reward | “range of z-scored decode >2: −104.5 ± 20.1 cm to +152.7 ± 22.9 cm” |\n   182→| GLM performance reference | FDE all place cells `0.10 ± 0.19`, TR `0.32 ± 0.13`, RR `0.29 ± 0.11`, non-RR `0.29 ± 0.11` | “On average, the model’s FDE…” |\n   183→\n   184→\n   185→### Processing Details\n   186→- Task structure:\n   187→  - hidden 50 cm reward zone on a 450 cm linear VR track\n   188→  - two main environments (`ENV 1`, `ENV 2`) in the switch task\n   189→  - reward locations A/B/C are equidistant and only one is active at a time\n   190→  - reward switches occur after 30 trials on switch days\n   191→  - first 10 trials of a new condition can receive automatic reward if the mouse fails to lick in the new zone\n   192→  - teleport period follows each lap: gray jitter period plus 50 cm tunnel before the next lap\n   193→- Imaging / alignment:\n   194→  - 2-photon CA1 calcium imaging with GCaMP7f\n   195→  - most sessions sampled at ~15.5 Hz\n   196→  - m17 and m18 were imaged in two planes interleaved at ~31 Hz, yielding ~15.5 Hz per plane\n   197→  - imaging sometimes stopped during teleport periods to minimize photodamage; some animals/days were imaged through teleports\n   198→- Neural preprocessing:\n   199→  - Suite2P used for motion correction and ROI detection\n   200→  - dF/F baseline computed independently within each trial using a `maximin` procedure with a 20 s sliding window\n   201→  - dF/F = `(F - baseline) / abs(baseline)`\n   202→  - dF/F smoothed with a two-sample Gaussian\n   203→  - deconvolved activity extracted with OASIS and used for decoder / SI analyses\n   204→- Behavioral / neural analysis conventions:\n   205→  - all spatial analyses exclude neural activity when speed `< 2 cm/s`\n   206→  - spatial binning is 45 bins of 10 cm each along the 450 cm track\n   207→  - licking is converted to binary counts and smoothed / binned for downstream analyses\n   208→- Decoder-specific paper processing:\n   209→  - decoder predicts circular reward-relative position from deconvolved activity\n   210→  - tenfold cross-validation, 90% train / 10% test\n   211→  - train/test either within pre-switch trials or train pre-switch and test post-switch\n   212→  - occupancy is downsampled to match reward-relative position bins\n   213→  - shuffled controls are generated by circularly shifting each cell’s timeseries\n   214→\n   215→### Curation Steps\n   216→\n   217→**Neuron curation rules**:\n   218→- Manual suite2p curation removed ROIs:\n   219→  - with multiple somata or dendrites\n   220→  - lacking visually obvious transients\n   221→  - suspected of calcium-indicator overexpression\n   222→  - with high continuous fluorescence fluctuation typical of putative interneurons\n   223→- Additional putative interneurons were excluded if Pearson correlation between dF/F and running speed exceeded `0.5`\n   224→- Reported additional exclusion from this speed-correlation rule: `0.42 ± 0.85%` of cells across mice/days\n   225→\n   226→**Trial curation rules**:\n   227→- Reward omission trials occur randomly on ~15% of trials and are retained as a task condition\n   228→- Licking analysis removes trials with erroneous lick detection when >30% of imaging frames in the trial have cumulative lick count `> 2`\n   229→- Reported lick-artifact removal: `81 / 12,376` imaged trials across 11 switch mice (~0.65%)\n   230→- Some analyses require at least three omission trials within a trial set\n   231→\n   232→### Decoders Trained\n   233→| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n   234→| Reward-relative position from deconvolved activity | Paper reports decode score above shuffle, not classification accuracy; after-switch generalization is significant only for RR cells |\n   235→| Single-neuron deconvolved activity from task + movement variables (GLM) | FDE: all place cells `0.10 ± 0.19`, TR `0.32 ± 0.13`, RR `0.29 ± 0.11`, non-RR `0.29 ± 0.11` |\n   236→\n   237→---\n   238→\n   239→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   240→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   241→\n   242→### Discrepancies Found\n   243→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n   244→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   245→| Cohort size | Code primarily analyzes the `MetaLearn` switch cohort by day, especially the seven switch days | NWB archive contains 11 mice / 152 subject-session files | Paper reports 11 switch-task mice plus a separate 3-mouse fixed-condition cohort | Treat the shared NWB archive as the 11-mouse switch cohort only. Fixed-condition mice are not present in `data/`. |\n   246→| Session count | Code expects one saved `sess` per imaging day; m11 has special handling in metadata | 152 NWB files = `11 * 14 - 2` | Paper states 14 task days, with imaging for m11 starting on day 3 | Counts are consistent once the m11 day 1-2 exception is applied. |\n   247→| Neural stream to decode | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` uses `sess.timeseries['events']` after dF/F + OASIS | NWB stores `Fluorescence`, `Neuropil`, and `Deconvolved`; no explicit dF/F timeseries | Paper decoder uses deconvolved calcium activity derived after per-trial dF/F | Use NWB `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/data` as the paper-equivalent neural signal. Raw `Fluorescence`/`Neuropil` remain available for sanity checks. |\n   248→| Cell filtering source | Code assumes suite2p curation has already been applied to the loaded `sess` data | NWB includes `iscell` in `PlaneSegmentation`, but segmentation tables for multipane mice contain ROIs not present in `Deconvolved/plane0/data` | Paper counts are post-curation putative pyramidal neurons | Filter cells by `iscell[:,0] > 0.5` only on ROI indices listed in `Deconvolved/plane0/rois`. This avoids overcounting plane-1 ROIs that are not in the response matrix. |\n   249→| Neurons/session | Code/paper operate on curated cells after ROI QC | Initial naive data count from full segmentation exceeded paper range; corrected ROI-referenced curated count is `155–1780` per session | Paper reports `155–2172` neurons/session | After using the ROI region correctly, the data are consistent with the paper’s reported range. |\n   250→| Multipane sessions | Code notes m17 and m18 were imaged in two planes and pooled for most analyses | NWB sessions for `sub-m17` and `sub-m18` have response rate `31.015625 Hz`, `planeIdx` includes `0/1`, but `Deconvolved/plane0/rois` selects only plane 0 cells | Paper states two-plane acquisition at ~31 Hz interleaved, ~15.5 Hz per plane | The archive exposes only the plane-0 response series for these files. Conversion will use the provided response matrix and document this archive-level limitation. |\n   251→| Trial boundary representation | Code uses `trial_start_inds` and `teleport_inds` arrays in `sess` | NWB provides framewise binary `trial_start` and `teleport` time series plus `trial number` | Paper defines laps/trials with a teleport period after each lap | Reconstruct trial start/end indices from `trial_start` and `teleport` signals in NWB. |\n   252→| Timebase | Code and methods emphasize ~15.5 Hz per plane | NWB contains both `15.5078125 Hz` and `31.015625 Hz` sessions | Paper says most sessions ~15.5 Hz and multipane mice interleaved at ~31 Hz for ~15.5 Hz/plane | Use a common output bin size during conversion so all sessions share the same decoder timebase. |\n   253→| Trial totals | Data inventory gives `12,216` starts / `12,217` unique trial numbers | NWB trial counts are slightly lower than one paper count | Paper cites `12,376` imaged trials across 11 switch mice in the lick-QC section and `80.5 ± 7.4` trials/session overall | Accept NWB-exported trial identities as ground truth for conversion, while using the paper’s mean trials/session as the stronger consistency check. The remaining ~1.3% mismatch is likely a paper-vs-export counting convention difference and will be tracked in later sanity checks. |\n   254→\n   255→---\n   256→\n   257→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   258→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   259→\n   260→### Variable Mapping\n   261→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   262→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   263→| `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/data` | `neural` | Crop to valid frame range, select ROI indices from `Deconvolved/plane0/rois`, keep curated cells by `iscell[:,0] > 0.5`, optionally exclude speed-correlated interneuron-like cells, transpose to `(neurons, time)`, split by trials, and rebin `31.015625 Hz` sessions to the common `15.5078125 Hz` bin size | `preprocessing.dff`, `utilities.multi_anim_sess`, `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` | Neural decoder signal should match paper deconvolved activity (`events`) as closely as possible. |\n   264→| `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/rois` + `processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation/iscell` | `neural` / `brain_region_idx` | Use ROI region referenced by response matrix, then `iscell[:,0] > 0.5` to keep curated cells only | suite2p curation assumption in `make_session_pkl.md`; `utilities.multi_anim_sess` | Critical for multipane sessions; do not use the full segmentation table. |\n   265→| `general/optophysiology/ImagingPlane/location` | `brain_regions` | Normalize `hippocampus, CA1` to `CA1` | N/A | All sessions are CA1 in this archive. |\n   266→| Reconstructed trial-relative elapsed time | `input[0]` | Seconds since trial start for each bin | trial handling implicit in `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` | Align all trials to the first frame of the trial. |\n   267→| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/environment/data` | `input[1]` | Per trial, take modal valid environment value (`0` or `1`) and repeat across bins | session/task logic in `behavior.get_trial_types` / paper task description | Map `0 -> ENV1`, `1 -> ENV2`. |\n   268→| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/trial number/data` | `input[2]` | Per trial, use within-session trial index and repeat across bins | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` creates 0-indexed `trial_ids` | Keep native 0-indexing to match code conventions. |\n   269→| Trial reward outcome shifted by one trial | `input[3]` | Previous trial rewarded (`1`) vs omitted (`0`), repeated across current-trial bins; first trial gets `0` | paper reward omission schedule; reward outcome from trial parsing | User-requested decoder input, not a paper decoder variable. |\n   270→| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/position/data` + inferred reward-zone bounds | `output[0]` | Signed distance to nearest point in active reward zone: negative before zone, `0` inside zone, positive after zone; discretize to 7 bins per task spec | reward-zone logic from `behavior.get_reward_zones`; reward-relative computations in `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` | For zone `[start, end]`: `pos < start -> pos-start`; `start <= pos <= end -> 0`; `pos > end -> pos-end`. |\n   271→| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/position/data` | `output[1]` | Discretize absolute position on the 450 cm corridor into 5 equal bins | paper: 45 spatial bins over 450 cm | Decoder spec requires 5 bins, not the paper’s 45 bins. |\n   272→| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/speed/data` | `output[2]` | Discretize speed into bins: `<2`, `2-10`, `10-20`, `20-40`, `>40 cm/s` | paper and code use speed threshold 2 cm/s | Keep all bins for output even though paper excludes `<2 cm/s` in some analyses. |\n   273→| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/lick/data` | `output[3]` | Convert to binary per bin (`lick > 0`) after lick-artifact handling | lick cleanup in `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`; methods licking QC | For rebinned 31 Hz sessions, use logical OR within each 15.5 Hz bin. |\n   274→| Inferred active reward zone per trial | `output[4]` | Map trial reward location to categorical `{A:0, B:1, C:2}` and repeat across trial bins | `behavior.get_reward_zones`; paper task design | Infer from reward-zone-active positions / reward events and fill omission trials within stable blocks. |\n   275→| Reward events assigned to trials | `output[5]` | Trial outcome categorical `{omitted:0, rewarded:1}` repeated across trial bins | `behavior.get_trial_types` | Rewarded if any `Reward` event timestamp falls within the trial. |\n   276→\n   277→### Key Decisions\n   278→1. **Use all 152 NWB sessions from the 11 switch-task mice**: The shared archive corresponds to the main switch cohort, not the 3 fixed-condition mice.\n   279→1. **Use NWB `Deconvolved` as the neural signal**: The paper’s decoder and SI analyses operate on deconvolved activity after dF/F, and this stream is already provided in the archive.\n   280→1. **Filter cells with ROI-region-aware `iscell` selection**: Only ROI ids referenced by the response matrix are valid candidates for inclusion.\n   281→1. **Plan to exclude putative interneuron-like cells**: The paper excludes cells with dF/F-speed correlation `> 0.5`, so the conversion script should implement this if feasible from the NWB fluorescence streams and reference `dff` logic.\n   282→1. **Reconstruct trials from `trial_start` and `teleport`**: This matches the reference trial definition more closely than relying only on trial-number changes.\n   283→1. **Standardize all sessions to a common `15.5078125 Hz` bin size**: This is the dominant dataset rate and the paper’s effective per-plane sampling rate.\n   284→1. **Use time-varying outputs for all decoder targets**: Even per-trial outputs (`reward zone`, `reward outcome`) will be repeated across bins for consistent `(n_output, n_timepoints)` arrays.\n   285→1. **Infer reward-zone location from rewarded trials and fill omission trials within stable blocks**: Missing zone labels in the NWB export are concentrated on omission trials; the task structure constrains the active zone to one contiguous block per condition.\n   286→1. **Use omission fraction and switch timing as sanity anchors**: Raw data show `rewarded = 84.65%`, `omission = 15.35%`, matching the paper’s ~15% omission rate, and switch sessions should change condition near trial 30.\n   287→1. **Keep within-session trial number 0-indexed**: This matches raw NWB values and `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`.\n   288→\n decoder model for circular variables; confirms the reference decoding was done on neural activity against position-like targets. |\n\n### Notes\n- The reference repo is for **2-photon calcium imaging**, not electrophysiology. There is no spike sorting or ephys quality filtering in this code path.\n- `sess` is the base aligned session object. The README explicitly states raw `sess` objects **do not contain dF/F**; dF/F is computed later.\n- `make_session_pkl.md` shows the first saved object is `sess`, created from raw suite2p + VR files after manual suite2p curation.\n- Cell curation is manual in the suite2p GUI; `iscell.npy` is the curated cell mask used when loading suite2p outputs.\n- `multi_anim_sess` is the main post-processing stage used by the paper:\n  - loads saved `sess` pickle for each animal/day,\n  - computes dF/F,\n  - optionally deconvolves dF/F into `events`,\n  - adds position-binned trial matrices,\n  - computes place-cell statistics and trial subset metadata.\n- For single-channel data, `multi_anim_sess(..., calc_spks=True)` calls `preprocessing.dff(...)` with:\n  - `neuropil_method='subtract'`\n  - `baseline_method='maximin'`\n  - `subtract_baseline=True`\n  - `neu_coef=0.7`\n  - `deconvolve=True`\n  - optional `keep_teleports` per animal\n- `dff` restricts valid fluorescence to trial spans using `trial_start_inds` and `teleport_inds`. Baseline is computed **within each trial** after smoothing, min-filtering, and max-filtering. The resulting dF/F is smoothed again, then OASIS is applied to get `events`.\n- `append_session_data` adds behavioral timeseries aligned to imaging frames. The pipeline is therefore based on **frame-aligned** neural and behavior streams.\n- `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` is the clearest reference for paper-style decoder inputs:\n  - neural data are `sess.timeseries['events']`\n  - trial boundaries use `trial_start_inds` and `teleport_inds`\n  - reward-relative position is computed per sample from the reward-zone start for that trial\n  - low-speed samples are masked out with `use_speed_thr=2`\n  - lick sensor artifacts are suppressed when cumulative licks are implausibly high\n  - samples with NaNs in neural data or masked speed/lick channels are dropped\n- Reward-zone labels come from `behavior.get_reward_zones`:\n  - `A` corresponds to zone `X` = `[80, 130]`\n  - `B` corresponds to zone `Y` = `[200, 250]`\n  - `C` corresponds to zone `Z` = `[320, 370]`\n  - environment names remain `Env1`, `Env2`, `Env3`\n- The Fig. 3 decoder notebook uses:\n  - experiment `MetaLearn`\n  - experiment days `[3, 5, 7, 8, 10, 12, 14]`\n  - `ts_key='dff'` when loading saved `multiDayData`, but continuous neural decoder inputs are assembled from `sess.timeseries['events']` via `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`\n  - occupancy matching by reward-relative position bin for some paper analyses\n- Conclusion for conversion:\n  - **Yes, dF/F must be computed** to match the reference processing.\n  - The decoder-relevant neural stream is most likely the **deconvolved `events`**, not raw fluorescence.\n  - Trial alignment should use `trial_start_inds` as the start and `teleport_inds` as the end of each trial.\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Task structure:\n  - hidden 50 cm reward zone on a 450 cm linear VR track\n  - two main environments (`ENV 1`, `ENV 2`) in the switch task\n  - reward locations A/B/C are equidistant and only one is active at a time\n  - reward switches occur after 30 trials on switch days\n  - first 10 trials of a new condition can receive automatic reward if the mouse fails to lick in the new zone\n  - teleport period follows each lap: gray jitter period plus 50 cm tunnel before the next lap\n- Imaging / alignment:\n  - 2-photon CA1 calcium imaging with GCaMP7f\n  - most sessions sampled at ~15.5 Hz\n  - m17 and m18 were imaged in two planes interleaved at ~31 Hz, yielding ~15.5 Hz per plane\n  - imaging sometimes stopped during teleport periods to minimize photodamage; some animals/days were imaged through teleports\n- Neural preprocessing:\n  - Suite2P used for motion correction and ROI detection\n  - dF/F baseline computed independently within each trial using a `maximin` procedure with a 20 s sliding window\n  - dF/F = `(F - baseline) / abs(baseline)`\n  - dF/F smoothed with a two-sample Gaussian\n  - deconvolved activity extracted with OASIS and used for decoder / SI analyses\n- Behavioral / neural analysis conventions:\n  - all spatial analyses exclude neural activity when speed `< 2 cm/s`\n  - spatial binning is 45 bins of 10 cm each along the 450 cm track\n  - licking is converted to binary counts and smoothed / binned for downstream analyses\n- Decoder-specific paper processing:\n  - decoder predicts circular reward-relative position from deconvolved activity\n  - tenfold cross-validation, 90% train / 10% test\n  - train/test either within pre-switch trials or train pre-switch and test post-switch\n  - occupancy is downsampled to match reward-relative position bins\n  - shuffled controls are generated by circularly shifting each cell’s timeseries\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n- Manual suite2p curation removed ROIs:\n  - with multiple somata or dendrites\n  - lacking visually obvious transients\n  - suspected of calcium-indicator overexpression\n  - with high continuous fluorescence fluctuation typical of putative interneurons\n- Additional putative interneurons were excluded if Pearson correlation between dF/F and running speed exceeded `0.5`\n- Reported additional exclusion from this speed-correlation rule: `0.42 ± 0.85%` of cells across mice/days\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Reward omission trials occur randomly on ~15% of trials and are retained as a task condition\n- Licking analysis removes trials with erroneous lick detection when >30% of imaging frames in the trial have cumulative lick count `> 2`\n- Reported lick-artifact removal: `81 / 12,376` imaged trials across 11 switch mice (~0.65%)\n- Some analyses require at least three omission trials within a trial set\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n| Reward-relative position from deconvolved activity | Paper reports decode score above shuffle, not classification accuracy; after-switch generalization is significant only for RR cells |\n| Single-neuron deconvolved activity from task + movement variables (GLM) | FDE: all place cells `0.10 ± 0.19`, TR `0.32 ± 0.13`, RR `0.29 ± 0.11`, non-RR `0.29 ± 0.11` |\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data Raw `Fluorescence`/`Neuropil` remain available for sanity checks. |\n| Cell filtering source | Code assumes suite2p curation has already been applied to the loaded `sess` data | NWB includes `iscell` in `PlaneSegmentation`, but segmentation tables for multipane mice contain ROIs not present in `Deconvolved/plane0/data` | Paper counts are post-curation putative pyramidal neurons | Filter cells by `iscell[:,0] > 0.5` only on ROI indices listed in `Deconvolved/plane0/rois`. This avoids overcounting plane-1 ROIs that are not in the response matrix. |\n| Neurons/session | Code/paper operate on curated cells after ROI QC | Initial naive data count from full segmentation exceeded paper range; corrected ROI-referenced curated count is `155–1780` per session | Paper reports `155–2172` neurons/session | After using the ROI region correctly, the data are consistent with the paper’s reported range. |\n| Multipane sessions | Code notes m17 and m18 were imaged in two planes and pooled for most analyses | NWB sessions for `sub-m17` and `sub-m18` have response rate `31.015625 Hz`, `planeIdx` includes `0/1`, but `Deconvolved/plane0/rois` selects only plane 0 cells | Paper states two-plane acquisition at ~31 Hz interleaved, ~15.5 Hz per plane | The archive exposes only the plane-0 response series for these files. Conversion will use the provided response matrix and document this archive-level limitation. |\n| Trial boundary representation | Code uses `trial_start_inds` and `teleport_inds` arrays in `sess` | NWB provides framewise binary `trial_start` and `teleport` time series plus `trial number` | Paper defines laps/trials with a teleport period after each lap | Reconstruct trial start/end indices from `trial_start` and `teleport` signals in NWB. |\n| Timebase | Code and methods emphasize ~15.5 Hz per plane | NWB contains both `15.5078125 Hz` and `31.015625 Hz` sessions | Paper says most sessions ~15.5 Hz and multipane mice interleaved at ~31 Hz for ~15.5 Hz/plane | Use a common output bin size during conversion so all sessions share the same decoder timebase. |\n| Trial totals | Data inventory gives `12,216` starts / `12,217` unique trial numbers | NWB trial counts are slightly lower than one paper count | Paper cites `12,376` imaged trials across 11 switch mice in the lick-QC section and `80.5 ± 7.4` trials/session overall | Accept NWB-exported trial identities as ground truth for conversion, while using the paper’s mean trials/session as the stronger consistency check. The remaining ~1.3% mismatch is likely a paper-vs-export counting convention difference and will be tracked in later sanity checks. |\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### rewarded:1}` repeated across trial bins | `behavior.get_trial_types` | Rewarded if any `Reward` event timestamp falls within the trial. |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Use all 152 NWB sessions from the 11 switch-task mice**: The shared archive corresponds to the main switch cohort, not the 3 fixed-condition mice.\n1. **Use NWB `Deconvolved` as the neural signal**: The paper’s decoder and SI analyses operate on deconvolved activity after dF/F, and this stream is already provided in the archive.\n1. **Filter cells with ROI-region-aware `iscell` selection**: Only ROI ids referenced by the response matrix are valid candidates for inclusion.\n1. **Plan to exclude putative interneuron-like cells**: The paper excludes cells with dF/F-speed correlation `> 0.5`, so the conversion script should implement this if feasible from the NWB fluorescence streams and reference `dff` logic.\n1. **Reconstruct trials from `trial_start` and `teleport`**: This matches the reference trial definition more closely than relying only on trial-number changes.\n1. **Standardize all sessions to a common `15.5078125 Hz` bin size**: This is the dominant dataset rate and the paper’s effective per-plane sampling rate.\n1. **Use time-varying outputs for all decoder targets**: Even per-trial outputs (`reward zone`, `reward outcome`) will be repeated across bins for consistent `(n_output, n_timepoints)` arrays.\n1. **Infer reward-zone location from rewarded trials and fill omission trials within stable blocks**: Missing zone labels in the NWB export are concentrated on omission trials; the task structure constrains the active zone to one contiguous block per condition.\n1. **Use omission fraction and switch timing as sanity anchors**: Raw data show `rewarded = 84.65%`, `omission = 15.35%`, matching the paper’s ~15% omission rate, and switch sessions should change condition near trial 30.\n1. **Keep within-session trial number 0-indexed**: This matches raw NWB values and `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Neural spot-check: for one single-plane session and one 31 Hz session, compare a raw NWB trial slice against converted `neural` with `np.allclose()` after ROI filtering and rebinning.\n- [ ] Input spot-check: verify `time_from_trial_start`, `environment`, `trial number`, and `previous outcome` on 3 hand-checked trials loaded confirm converted trial counts match reconstructed `trial_start` / `teleport` boundaries.\n- [ ] Cohort statistics check: converted subjects/sessions/trials/neurons must match the NWB inventory and remain within paper-reported ranges.\n- [ ] Outcome distribution check: rewarded/omission fraction in converted data should remain close to the raw-data fraction (`84.65% / 15.35%`).\n\n---\n\n## Step Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n    20→- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference paper and code repository. \n    21→- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n    22→- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \n    23→\n    24→## Reference Information\n    25→\n    26→- The reference **paper** \"A flexible hippocampal population code for experience relative to reward\" is in the file `paper.pdf`.\n    27→- Some parts of the paper that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \n    28→- **Code** from the paper are in the directory `code`.\n    29→- **Data** from the paper are in the directory `data`.\n    30→\n    31→## Decoder Task\n    32→\n    33→Temporally align based on start of the trial. \n    34→\n    35→### Decoder Inputs:\n    36→- Time from start of trial in seconds (continuous, time-varying)\n    37→- Environment type (binary, ENV1 vs ENV2, per trial)\n    38→- Trial number (continuous, per trial)\n    39→- Previous trial outcome (binary, omitted = 0, rewarded = 1, per trial)\n    40→\n    41→### Decoder Outputs\n    42→- Distance to any location in the reward zone, time-varying. Discretization bins:\n    43→  - 0:  < -50 cm\n    44→  - 1: -50 to -10 cm\n    45→  - 2: -10 cm to < 0 cm\n    46→  - 3: 0 cm\n    47→  - 4: >0 cm to +10 cm\n    48→  - 5: +10 to +50 cm\n    49→  - 6: > +50 cm\n    50→- Absolute position in corridor, discretized into 5 equal-sized bins, time-varying\n    51→- Speed, time-varying. Discretized into bins:\n    52→  - 0: < 2 cm/s\n    53→  - 1: 2-10 cm/s\n    54→  - 2: 10-20 cm/s\n    55→  - 3: 20-40 cm/s\n    56→  - 4: > 40 cm/s\n    57→- Lick, time-varying. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n    58→- Reward zone location, per-trial. 0 = A, 1 = B, 2 = C\n    59→- Reward outcome, per-trial. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n    60→\n    61→## Target Data Format\n    62→\n    63→- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n    64→- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n    65→\n    66→```python\n    67→data = {\n    68→    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n    69→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    70→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n    71→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n    72→            ...\n    73→        ],\n    74→        [  # list of trials for session 2\n    75→            ...\n    76→        ],\n    77→        ...\n    78→    ],\n    79→\n    80→    'input': [  # list of sessions\n    81→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    82→            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n    83→            ...\n    84→        ],\n    85→        ...\n    86→    ],\n    87→\n    88→    'output': [  # list of sessions\n    89→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    90→            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n    91→            ...\n    92→        ],\n    93→        ...\n    94→    ],\n    95→\n    96→    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    97→    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n    98→\n    99→    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n   100→    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n   101→        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n   102→    ] # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n   103→\n   104→    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n   105→    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n   106→    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n   107→        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n   108→        ...\n   109→    ],\n   110→\n   111→    'metadata': {\n   112→        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n   113→        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n   114→        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n   115→        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   116→        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   117→        ...\n   118→    }\n   119→}\n   120→```\n   121→\n   122→### Key Format Requirements\n   123→\n   124→- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n   125→  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n   126→  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n   127→\n   128→- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n   129→  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n   130→  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics such as:\n   131→    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n   132→    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n   133→  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n   134→  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n   135→  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n   136→\n   137→- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n   138→  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n   139→  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n   140→  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n   141→  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n   142→  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n   143→\n   144→- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n   145→- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n   146→  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n   147→\n   148→- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n   149→- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n   150→\n   151→- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n   152→  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n   153→\n   154→- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n   155→  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n   156→- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n   157→  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n   158→\n   159→- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n   160→  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n   161→  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n   162→  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n   163→  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n   164→  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n   165→\n   166→## Python environment\n   167→\n   168→- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n   169→- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n   170→- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n   171→\n   172→## Decoder Reference\n   173→\n   174→- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n   175→- Optional arguments:\n   176→  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n   177→  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n   178→  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n   179→- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n   180→\n   181→### Purpose\n   182→\n   183→These functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n   184→1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   185→2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   186→3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   187→4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n   188→5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n   189→\n   190→### Consistency requirements\n   191→\n   192→Your processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n   193→- Loading of data\n   194→- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n   195→- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n   196→- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n   197→\n   198→To check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n   199→\n   200→Whenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n   201→\n   202→Discrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above. \n   203→\n   204→Carefully verify your work after every step. When you find mistakes, document and fix them immediately. Be critical of results **after every step of processing**. \n   205→\n   206→You will be assessed on whether the decisions you make on how to load, filter, process, align, reformat, and save the data are reasonable and match the reference paper and code and these instructions. During testing, the reference version of `train_decoder.py` will be run on your converted data. Data statistics and decoder accuracy will be compared to those achieved by human-written conversion code. \n   207→\n   208→---\n   209→\n   210→## Conversion Workflow\n   211→\n   212→**IMPORTANT**: Follow these steps in order. Save plots as `processing_<session_id>.png`.\n   363→- Reuse code from reference code bases where appropriate\n   364→- Write efficient code:\n   365→  - Vectorize loops\n   366→  - Avoid unnecessary file I/O\n   367→  - Use parallel processing if beneficial\n   368→  - Print timing information to find bottlenecks\n   369→- Plots for `--show-processing` mode should visually convince the user that every step of the conversion is correct.\n   370→  - There are no temporal misalignments\n   371→  - Discretization of continuous outputs is correct\n   372→- Write clear, well-documented functions\n   373→- Handle missing data appropriately (consult references, use sensible defaults, document)\n   374→- Validate data shapes and types at each step\n   375→- Include sanity checks (e.g., trial counts match across arrays)\n   376→\n   377→**Done when**: `convert_data.py` exists and runs efficiently without errors.\n   378→\n   379→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 7 until `convert_data.py` exists and you have updated Step 6 in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   380→\n   381→---\n   382→\n   383→### Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   384→\n   385→**Goal**: Validate the conversion on a small sample before processing the full dataset.\n   386→\n   387→**Actions**:\n   388→1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_full_out.txt`. \n   445→6. Check that no data was lost during conversion \n   446→7. Spot-check a few sessions to verify data integrity\n   447→8. **Check for consistency** between dataset statistics in `verification_full_out.txt` and the reference texts\n   448→9. Investigate any inconsistencies, and revise the conversion script until all dataset statistics are consistent\n   449→\n   450→**Done when**:\n   451→- `converted_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n   452→- `verification_full_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n   453→- **ALL** dataset statistics are consistent with values from reference texts\n   454→- You have documented statistics and consistency in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 9\"\n   455→\n   456→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 10 until `converted_data.pkl` and `verification_full_out.txt` files exist and CONVERSION_NOTES.MD Step 9 is filled in.\n   457→\n   458→---\n   459→\n   460→### Step 10: Critical Review 1\n   461→\n   462→**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Explain why each warning could not be fixed in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   466→\n   467→**Check 2: Construct sanity checks**\n   468→- Develop at least one sanity check each on neural, input, and output data.\n   469→- These must involve loading in the original data files (NOT through your conversion code).\n   470→- Construct comparison tests and criteria using `np.allclose()`. \n   471→- These tests can be spot-checks of the data, e.g. neural activity at trial 5, timepoint 10, neuron 3. \n   472→- Fix all mismatches/sanity check failures. \n   473→- Document your sanity checks to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   474→\n   475→**Check 3: Reference code comparison**\n   476→- Compare your code to the reference code and methods. \n   477→- For each major processing step — (a) data loading, (b) neuron/trial filtering, (c) temporal alignment, (d) binning, (e) input construction, (f) output construction — identify the corresponding code in your script and in the reference, and compare the logic. \n   478→- Check whether you use the same variables, filtering, and processing as the reference code. \n   479→- Fix any mismatches. \n   480→- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   481→- Explain your reasoning for any differences in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`.\n   482→\n   483→**Check 4: Key statistics comparison**\n   484→- Verify that key statistics (number of animals, sessions, trials, neurons, input and output ranges, output distributions) match information available in the reference paper. \n   485→- Write code to investigate any discrepancies. \n   486→- Fix any discovered issues. \n   487→- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   488→\n   489→**Check 5: Check for edge cases**\n   490→- Check for off-by-one errors at the beginning/ends of trials, sessions, and subjects, or in definitions of the inputs and outputs. \n   491→- There may be minor issues in the data that your code must handle. Check that your code robustly handles issues in the original data. \n   492→\n   493→**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n   494→1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n   495→2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n   496→3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n   497→4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n   498→\n   499→Write a report to CONVERSION_NOTES.md describing **every** check you did, to help convince the user that the conversion code works. **Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 10\".\n   500→\n   501→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 11 until you have checked for consistency between **every** statistic in the reference texts, code, and data and documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Step 10. \n   502→\n   503→---\n   504→\n   505→### Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n   506→\n   507→**Goal**: Validate the decoder on the **complete** dataset.\n   508→\n   509→**⚠️ IMPORTANT**: This step must complete fully. Do not skip or abbreviate. Do not proceed to Step 12 until this step is completely finished.\n   510→\n   511→**Actions**:\n   512→1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --plot-samples 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_full_out.txt`. If the GPU does not have sufficient RAM for the network training, use the flag `--cpu` to specify to use the CPU to train. \n   513→2. **Wait for complete execution** — this may take significant time for large datasets\n   514→3. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n   515→4. **Check accuracy**: High accuracy is an indicator that data conversion has been done correctly. Do this BEFORE exploring any code or data. Document everything in this file as you work.\n\n2. **FOLLOW STEPS IN ORDER**: Do not skip steps. Do not proceed to step N+1 until step N is complete. Each step has \"Done when\" criteria — verify them before moving on.\n\n3. **MATCH THE REFERENCE PROCESSING**: Your processing must match what's described in the reference paper and code. You will be assessed on this consistency.\n\n---\n\n## Project Context\n\n- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference paper and code repository. \n- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \n\n## Reference Information\n\n- The reference **paper** \"A flexible hippocampal population code for experience relative to reward\" is in the file `paper.pdf`.\n- Some parts of the paper that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \n- **Code** from the paper are in the directory `code`.\n- **Data** from the paper are in the directory `data`.\n\n## Decoder Task\n\nTemporally align based on start of the trial. \n\n### Decoder Inputs:\n- Time from start of trial in seconds (continuous, time-varying)\n- Environment type (binary, ENV1 vs ENV2, per trial)\n- Trial number (continuous, per trial)\n- Previous trial outcome (binary, omitted = 0, rewarded = 1, per trial)\n\n### Decoder Outputs\n- Distance to any location in the reward zone, time-varying. Discretization bins:\n  - 0:  < -50 cm\n  - 1: -50 to -10 cm\n  - 2: -10 cm to < 0 cm\n  - 3: 0 cm\n  - 4: >0 cm to +10 cm\n  - 5: +10 to +50 cm\n  - 6: > +50 cm\n- Absolute position in corridor, discretized into 5 equal-sized bins, time-varying\n- Speed, time-varying. Discretized into bins:\n  - 0: < 2 cm/s\n  - 1: 2-10 cm/s\n  - 2: 10-20 cm/s\n  - 3: 20-40 cm/s\n  - 4: > 40 cm/s\n- Lick, time-varying. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n- Reward zone location, per-trial. 0 = A, 1 = B, 2 = C\n- Reward outcome, per-trial. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n\n## Target Data Format\n\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n\n```python\ndata = {\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    ] # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'metadata': {\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Python environment\n\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n\n### Consistency requirements\n\nYour processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n- Loading of data\n- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n\nTo check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n\nWhenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n\nDiscrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above. \n\nCarefully verify your work after every step. When you find mistakes, document and fix them immediately. Be critical of results **after every step of processing**. \n\nYou will be assessed on whether the decisions you make on how to load, filter, process, align, reformat, and save the data are reasonable and match the reference paper and code and these instructions. Do NOT look outside this project directory.\n\n**Goal**: Understand the native data structure\n\n**Actions**:\n- Identify how data is organized (all files, formats, hierarchies)\n- Determine available variables and their meanings\n- Check data dimensions and data types\n- Look for documentation or README files\n- Note the data organization and structure\n- Note dataset size parameters such as the **total number** of subjects (mice), sessions, trials, and neurons\n\n**Done when**: You have documented the data structure, file formats, size, and available variables in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 2\".\n\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 3, update Step 2 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n\n---\n\n### Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n\n**Goal**: Extract key information from the paper and methods.\n\n**Actions**:\n- Scan the reference papers and methods.txt for important details about the data\n- Look for and note to file:\n  - Any information about dataset size (number of neurons, subjects, sessions, trials)\n  - Fraction of data with different values, e.g. fraction of rewarded outcomes\n  - Data processing, curation, and filtering\n  - Variables important to experiments\n  - Temporal alignment\n  - Temporal binning\n- Read the reference paper to find information about expected decoder accuracy.\n\n**Done when**: You have documented expected dataset statistics and processing details in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 3\".\n\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 4, update Step 3 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n\n---\n\n### Step 4: Check for Consistency\n\n**Goal**: Verify your understanding is consistent across all sources. \n\n**Actions**:\n- Compare your understanding of:\n  - Important functions from exploring the reference code\n  - Dataset structure from exploring the dataset\n  - Analysis described in the reference texts\n- Note and investigate any discrepancies\n- In particular, look for discrepancies in:\n  - Experimental and behavioral variables\n  - Data processing\n  - Data curation\n- Iterate until your understanding of the reference code, dataset, and reference texts are consistent\n\n**Done when**: You have resolved all discrepancies and documented your final understanding in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 4\".\n\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 5, update Step 4 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n\n---\n\n### Step 5: Mapping Planning\n\n**Goal**: Create a detailed plan for mapping source data to target format.\n\n**Actions**:\n- Map source data elements to target format components:\n  - Which variables contain neural activity?\n  - Identify ALL available experimental variables\n  - Which variables should be decoder **inputs** vs **outputs**? (Follow Decoder Task section)\n  - How to discretize continuous outputs (Follow Decoder Task section)\n  - Which inputs/outputs should be **time-varying** vs **per-trial** (Follow Decoder Task section)\n  - How does the reference code load and manipulate this data?\n  - When possible, import or copy code from the reference code in `code` directory. \n- Consult CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4 and reference materials to determine:\n  - Temporal binning for spike rates (if applicable)\n  - Temporal alignment of trials\n  - Data filtering and curation rules for neurons, trials, and sessions\n  - Brain regions recorded from\n- Mapping should be consistent with CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4.\n- Check for variables indicating valid data periods — exclude invalid data\n- Note all decisions, edge cases, and data quality issues\n- Plan and document **sanity and consistency checks** based on dataset size information from the reference texts, code, and data\n\n**Done when**:\n- You have a complete mapping document in CONVERSION_NOTES.md specifying every source-to-target variable mapping under \"Step 5\"\n- You have documented all decisions under \"Step 5\"\n- You have documented sanity and consistency checks under \"Step 5\"\n\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 6, update Step 5 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n\n---\n\n### Step 6: Script Development\n\n**Goal**: Write the conversion script to `convert_data.py`.\n\n**Actions**:\n- Write all conversion code in the file `convert_data.py`\n- Script should run as **`python -u convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`**\n- Include options:\n  - `--full` (default): Process all sessions\n  - `--sample`: Process only 2 sessions for testing\n  - `--show-processing`: Plot visualizations of EVERY processing step for up to 2 sessions. Save plots as `processing_<session_id>.png`.\n- Reuse code from reference code bases where appropriate\n- Write efficient code:\n  - Vectorize loops\n  - Avoid unnecessary file I/O\n  - Use parallel processing if beneficial\n  - Print timing information to find bottlenecks\n- Plots for `--show-processing` mode should visually convince the user that every step of the conversion is correct.\n  - There are no temporal misalignments\n  - Discretization of continuous outputs is correct\n- Write clear, well-documented functions\n- Handle missing data appropriately (consult references, use sensible defaults, document)\n- Validate data shapes and types at each step\n- Include sanity checks (e.g., trial counts match across arrays)\n\n**Done when**: `convert_data.py` exists and runs efficiently without errors.\n\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 7 until `convert_data.py` exists and you have updated Step 6 in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n\n---\n\n### Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n\n**Goal**: Validate the conversion on a small sample before processing the full dataset.\n\n**Actions**:\n1. Run `python -u convert_data.py sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing 2>&1 | tee conversion_sample_out.txt`\n  a. Verify the sample data structure matches the specification.\n  b. Check dimensions are consistent across trials/sessions.\n  c. Check that no data is missed during conversion. \n  d. Validate that metadata accurately describes the data.\n2. Improve efficiency of conversion.\n  a. Look at and note timing information for the conversion. \n  b. Estimate how long the full conversion will run. When you do this, make sure that your estimate accounts for differences in lengths/numbers of trials/sessions. \n  c. If full conversion time estimate is longer than 15 minutes, speed up the code by writing more efficient code (vectorizing loops, removing unnecessary or redundant computations) and/or including parallel processing. \n3. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_sample_out.txt`.\n4. Analyze the output file `verification_sample_out.txt`:\n  a. Verify no errors reported.\n  b. Attempt to address any warnings\n  c. Check input ranges and output value distributions against expectations from reference texts.\n\n**Done when**: \n- `sample_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n- `verification_sample_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n- Processing plots show no anomalies\n- Document sample statistics in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 7\"\n\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 8 until `sample_data.pkl` and `verification_sample_out.txt` files exist, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 7 is filled in. \n\n---\n\n### Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n\n**Goal**: Verify the decoder runs successfully on sample data.\n\n**Actions**:\n1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_sample_out.txt`\n2. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n3. **Check accuracy**: Verify accuracy is above chance for EVERY output\n\n**Investigate any issues**:\n- If accuracy is low: check for conversion bugs or reconsider output representation\n\n**Done when**: \n- Decoder training completes without errors\n- Loss decreases over epochs\n- Accuracies are above chance\n- Document all results in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 8\"\n\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 9 until `train_decoder_sample_out.txt` file exists, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 8 is filled in. \n\n---\n\n### Step 9: Full Conversion and Validation\n\n**Goal**: Process the complete dataset.\n\n**Actions**:\n1. Review your time estimate from Step 7.\n2. If estimated time is long (e.g. longer than 15 minutes), optimize bottlenecks first.\n3. Run `python -u convert_data.py converted_data.pkl --full 2>&1 | tee conversion_full_out.txt`\n4. As the code runs, update your estimates of how long processing will take. If it is much longer than your previous estimate (> 1.5x), kill the process, optimize bottlenecks, and repeat. \n5. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_full_out.txt`. \n6. Check that no data was lost during conversion \n7. Spot-check a few sessions to verify data integrity\n8. **Check for consistency** between dataset statistics in `verification_full_out.txt` and the reference texts\n9. Investigate any inconsistencies, and revise the conversion script until all dataset statistics are consistent\n\n**Done when**:\n- `converted_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n- `verification_full_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n- **ALL** dataset statistics are consistent with values from reference texts\n- You have documented statistics and consistency in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 9\"\n\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 10 until `converted_data.pkl` and `verification_full_out.txt` files exist and CONVERSION_NOTES.MD Step 9 is filled in.\n\n---\n\n### Step 10: Critical Review 1\n\n**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain.\n\n**Check 1: Output log verification**\nRead through `verification_full_out.txt` to find and fix **all errors**. Address each warning by either fixing its cause or verifying it that it is not possible to fix it. Explain why each warning could not be fixed in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n\n**Check 2: Construct sanity checks**\n- Develop at least one sanity check each on neural, input, and output data.\n- These must involve loading in the original data files (NOT through your conversion code).\n- Construct comparison tests and criteria using `np.allclose()`. \n- These tests can be spot-checks of the data, e.g. neural activity at trial 5, timepoint 10, neuron 3. \n- Fix all mismatches/sanity check failures. \n- Document your sanity checks to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n\n**Check 3: Reference code comparison**\n- Compare your code to the reference code and methods. \n- For each major processing step — (a) data loading, (b) neuron/trial filtering, (c) temporal alignment, (d) binning, (e) input construction, (f) output construction — identify the corresponding code in your script and in the reference, and compare the logic. \n- Check whether you use the same variables, filtering, and processing as the reference code. \n- Fix any mismatches. \n- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n- Explain your reasoning for any differences in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`.\n\n**Check 4: Key statistics comparison**\n- Verify that key statistics (number of animals, sessions, trials, neurons, input and output ranges, output distributions) match information available in the reference paper. \n- Write code to investigate any discrepancies. \n- Fix any discovered issues. \n- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n\n**Check 5: Check for edge cases**\n- Check for off-by-one errors at the beginning/ends of trials, sessions, and subjects, or in definitions of the inputs and outputs. \n- There may be minor issues in the data that your code must handle. Check that your code robustly handles issues in the original data. \n\n**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n\nWrite a report to CONVERSION_NOTES.md describing **every** check you did, to help convince the user that the conversion code works. **Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 10\".\n\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 11 until you have checked for consistency between **every** statistic in the reference texts, code, and data and documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Step 10. \n\n---\n\n### Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n\n**Goal**: Validate the decoder on the **complete** dataset.\n\n**⚠️ IMPORTANT**: This step must complete fully. Do not skip or abbreviate. Do not proceed to Step 12 until this step is completely finished.\n\n**Actions**:\n1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --plot-samples 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_full_out.txt`. If the GPU does not have sufficient RAM for the network training, use the flag `--cpu` to specify to use the CPU to train. \n2. **Wait for complete execution** — this may take significant time for large datasets\n3. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n4. **Check accuracy**: High accuracy is an indicator that data conversion has been done correctly. Accuracy near chance is a sign that there might be issues in temporal alignment of signals, choice of data streams, or filtering or processing of data. Compare accuracy to expectations based on the paper. \n\n**Done when**: \n1. Full decoder training completes (the script finishes running)\n2. Accuracy results are documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 11\" with a table of accuracies\n\n---\n\n### Step 12: Critical Review 2\n\n**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain. Perform all of the following checks:\n\n**Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis**\n- For each output, compare validation accuracy to chance (1/num_classes)\n- Accuracy below chance indicates a bug, investigate and fix conversion issues to improve this. \n- Accuracy below 1.5x chance may also indicate a bug, investigate whether changes in conversion logic could improve accuracy. \n\n**Check 2: Accuracy comparison to paper**\n- Find every decoding accuracy reported in the reference paper\n- Create a table comparing your accuracy to the paper's accuracy for each variable\n- If your accuracy is lower than the reported accuracy, this may indicate a bug in your conversion that you must fix. \n- Differences in algorithm/architecture should be relatively small because of the data dimensionality and size. Do not use this as an excuse to dismiss differences, investigate them completely before moving on. \n\n**Check 3: Train vs validation gap**\n- If training accuracy is much higher (>1.5x) than validation accuracy for any output, investigate overfitting or data leakage. \n\n**If accuracy is low for an output, try these specific debugging steps:**\n1. Verify the output values are correct by loading raw data and checking 3 specific trials\n2. Check temporal alignment — plot neural + output for a single trial to verify they're synchronized\n3. Check whether the output has enough variation (not 99% one class)\n4. Check that filtering steps for neural activity streams are followed. \n5. Check that processing of the data matches the reference code and paper. \n\n**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n\n**Report all checks and results** to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` to convince the user that the conversion and decoding has been done correctly. \n\n**Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 12\".\n\n---\n\n### Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\n\n**Goal**: Finalize documentation and organize files.\n\n**Actions**:\n- Ensure CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete with all:\n  - Key decisions and rationale\n  - Checks for correctness of the conversion\n  - Validation results and tables\n  - Any issues found and how they were resolved\n- Create a user-facing `README.md` summarizing:\n  - Dataset description\n  - How to load and use the converted data\n  - Output format specification\n  - Key statistics\n- Move analysis/investigation scripts to a `cache/` folder\n- Create `cache/README_CACHE.md` documenting cached files\n\n**Done when**: README.md exists, CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete, and directory is clean.\n\n---\n\n## CONVERSION_NOTES.md Template\n\n**Create this file IMMEDIATELY in Step 0. out[(distance_cm > 0.0) & (distance_cm <= 10.0)] = 4\n    out[(distance_cm > 10.0) & (distance_cm <= 50.0)] = 5\n    out[distance_cm > 50.0] = 6\n    return out\n\n\ndef lick=beh[\"lick/data\"][()][:t_common].astype(np.float32, copy=False),\n            environment=beh[\"environment/data\"][()][:t_common].astype(np.float32, copy=False),\n            reward_zone_signal=beh[\"reward_zone/data\"][()][:t_common].astype(np.float32, copy=False),\n            scanning=beh[\"scanning/data\"][()][:t_common].astype(np.float32, copy=False),\n            trial_number=beh[\"trial number/data\"][()][:t_common].astype(np.float32, copy=False),\n            trial_start=beh[\"trial_start/data\"][()][:t_common].astype(np.float32, copy=False),\n            teleport=beh[\"teleport/data\"][()][:t_common].astype(np.float32, copy=False),\n            reward_times=beh[\"Reward/timestamps\"][()].astype(np.float64, copy=False),\n            neural=neural[:t_common],\n            roi_ids=roi_ids,\n        )\n\n\ndef reward_frames_for_session(arrays: SessionArrays) -> np.ndarray:\n    if arrays.reward_times.size == 0:\n        return np.empty((0,), dtype=np.int64)\n    idx = np.searchsorted(arrays.timestamps, arrays.reward_times, side=\"left\")\n    return np.clip(idx, 0, arrays.timestamps.shape[0] - 1).astype(np.int64, copy=False)\n\n\ndef plot_processing_summary(\n    session_label: str,\n    trial_env: np.ndarray,\n    trial_zone: np.ndarray,\n    trial_rewarded: np.ndarray,\n    example_position: np.ndarray,\n    example_speed: np.ndarray,\n    example_lick: np.ndarray,\n    example_output: np.ndarray,\n    example_neural: np.ndarray,\n    original_rate_hz: float,\n    output_path: str,\n) -> None:\n    fig, fig.savefig(output_path, dpi=150)\n    plt.close(fig)\n\n\ndef convert_session(\n    arrays: SessionArrays,\n    show_processing: bool,\n) -> tuple[list[np.ndarray], list[np.ndarray], list[np.ndarray], np.ndarray, dict]:\n    t0 = time.perf_counter()\n\n    trial_slices = build_trial_slices(arrays.trial_start, arrays.teleport)\n    reward_frame_idx = arrays.lick[start:stop_exclusive]\n        if lick_trial.size and np.mean(lick_trial > 2) > LICK_ARTIFACT_FRACTION:\n            lick_artifact[trial_id] = True\n\n    trial_zone, trial_env=trial_env,\n    )\n\n    if math.isclose(arrays.rate_hz, TARGET_RATE_HZ):\n        factor = 1\n    elif math.isclose(arrays.rate_hz, 2.0 * TARGET_RATE_HZ):\n        factor = 2\n    else:\n        raise RuntimeError(f\"Unexpected sampling rate {arrays.rate_hz} in {arrays.session_label}\")\n\n    neural_trials: list[np.ndarray] = []\n    input_trials: list[np.ndarray] = []\n    output_trials: list[np.ndarray] = []\n\n    for trial_id, (start, stop_exclusive) in enumerate(trial_slices):\n        if lick_artifact[trial_id]:\n            continue\n\n        neural_trial = lick_binary.astype(np.int64, copy=False)\n\n        t_bins = neural_trial.shape[1]\n        time_from_start = (np.arange(t_bins, dtype=np.float32) * TARGET_DT_S)\n        env_series = np.full((t_bins,), float(trial_env[trial_id]), dtype=np.float32)\n        trial_series = np.full((t_bins,), float(trial_id), dtype=np.float32)\n        prev_reward_series = np.full((t_bins,), float(trial_prev_rewarded[trial_id]), dtype=np.float32)\n\n        zone_idx = int(trial_zone[trial_id])\n        dist = compute_distance_to_zone(position, zone_idx)\n        dist_bin = discretize_distance(dist)\n        pos_bin = discretize_position(position)\n        speed_bin = discretize_speed(speed)\n        zone_bin = np.full((t_bins,), zone_idx, dtype=np.int64)\n        reward_bin = np.full((t_bins,), int(trial_rewarded[trial_id]), dtype=np.int64)\n\n        input_trial = np.stack(\n            [time_from_start, env_series, trial_series, prev_reward_series],\n            axis=0,\n        ).astype(np.float32, copy=False)\n        output_trial = np.stack(\n            [dist_bin, pos_bin, speed_bin, lick_binary, zone_bin, reward_bin],\n            axis=0,\n        ).astype(np.int64, copy=False)\n\n        neural_trials.append(neural_trial.astype(np.float32, copy=False))\n        input_trials.append(input_trial)\n        output_trials.append(output_trial)\n\n \"n_trials_raw\": len(trial_slices),\n        \"n_trials_kept\": len(neural_trials),\n        \"n_trials_lick_artifact_removed\": int(lick_artifact.sum()),\n        \"n_neurons_kept\": (arrays.lick[trial_slices[example_idx][0] : trial_slices[example_idx][1]] > 0).astype(np.int64),\n                factor,\n            ),\n            example_output=output_trials[example_idx],\n            example_neural=neural_trials[example_idx],\n            original_rate_hz=arrays.rate_hz,\n            output_path=f\"processing_{arrays.session_label}.png\",\n        )\n\n    brain_region_idx = between input/output variables based on range similarity.\n\n    Args:\n        submitted_ranges: dict of {name: [min, max]}\n        reference_ranges: dict of {name: [min, max]}\n\n    Returns:\n        matches: list of (ref_name, sub_name, cost) tuples\n        cost_matrix: the full cost matrix\n    \"\"\"\n    ref_names = list(reference_ranges.keys())\n    sub_names = reference_fractions):\n    \"\"\"Find 1-1 matching between outputs: ranges must match exactly, then minimize fraction error.\n\n    Cost is inf if ranges don't match, otherwise mean absolute difference of sorted fractions.\n\n    Args:\n        submitted_ranges: dict of {name: [min, max]}\n        reference_ranges: dict of {name: [min, max]}\n        submitted_fractions: dict of {name: {val: frac, ...}}\n        reference_fractions: dict of {name: {val: frac, ...}}\n\n    Returns:\n        matches: list of (ref_name, sub_name, cost) tuples\n        cost_matrix: the full cost
