enumerate(ref_names):\n        for j, sn in enumerate(sub_names):\n            ref_lo, ref_hi = reference_ranges[rn]\n            sub_lo, sub_hi = submitted_ranges[sn]\n            # ranges must match exactly\n            if ref_lo != sub_lo or ref_hi != sub_hi:\n                continue\n            # compare fraction distributions using sorted values\n            ref_fracs = sorted(reference_fractions[rn].values())\n            sub_fracs = sorted(submitted_fractions[sn].values())\n            if len(ref_fracs) != len(sub_fracs):\n                continue\n int:\n   112→    if values.size == 0:\n   113→        return default\n   114→    values = values.astype(np.int64, copy=False)\n   115→    values = values[values >= 0]\n   116→    if values.size == 0:\n   117→        return default\n   118→    return int(np.bincount(values).argmax())\n   119→\n   120→\n   121→def infer_sample_files(files: list[str]) -> list[str]:\n   122→    metadata = []\n             chosen.append(path)\n   142→            if len(chosen) == 2:\n   143→                break\n   144→\n   145→    return chosen[:2]\n   146→\n   147→\n   148→def select_session_files(files: 159→    if factor == 1:\n   160→        return x.astype(np.float32, copy=False)\n   161→    n_full = x.shape[0] // factor\n   162→    out = []\n   163→    if n_full:\n   164→        out.append(x[: n_full * factor].reshape(n_full, factor).mean(axis=1))\n   165→    if x.shape[0] % factor:\n   166→        out.append(np.array([x[n_full * factor :].mean()], dtype=np.float32))\n   167→    return np.concatenate(out).astype(np.float32, copy=False)\n   168→\n   169→\n   170→def rebin_1d_any(x: np.ndarray, factor: int) -> np.ndarray:\n   171→    if factor == 1:\n   172→        return x.astype(np.int64, copy=False)\n   173→    n_full = x.shape[0] // factor\n   174→    out = []\n   175→    if n_full:\n   176→        out.append(np.any(x[: n_full * factor].reshape(n_full, factor) > 0, axis=1))\n   177→    if x.shape[0] % factor:\n   178→        out.append(np.array([np.any(x[n_full * factor :] > 0)], dtype=bool))\n   179→    return np.concatenate(out).astype(np.int64, copy=False)\n   180→\n   181→\n   182→def rebin_2d_sum_time_last(x: np.ndarray, factor: int) -> np.ndarray:\n   183→    if factor == 1:\n   184→        return x.astype(np.float32, copy=False)\n   185→    t = x.shape[1]\n   186→    n_full = t // factor\n   187→    parts = []\n   188→    if n_full:\n   189→        full = x[:, : n_full * factor].reshape(x.shape[0], n_full, factor).sum(axis=2)\n   190→        parts.append(full)\n   191→    if t % factor:\n   192→        parts.append(x[:, n_full * factor :].sum(axis=1, keepdims=True))\n   193→    return np.concatenate(parts, axis=1).astype(np.float32, copy=False)\n   194→\n   195→\n   196→def discretize_distance(distance_cm: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n   197→    out = np.zeros(distance_cm.shape, dtype=np.int64)\n   198→    out[distance_cm < -50.0] = 0\n   199→    out[(distance_cm >= -50.0) & (distance_cm < -10.0)] = 1\n   200→    out[(distance_cm >= -10.0) & (distance_cm < 0.0)] = 2\n   201→    out[distance_cm == 0.0] = 3\n   202→    out[(distance_cm > 0.0) & (distance_cm <= 10.0)] = 4\n   203→    out[(distance_cm > 10.0) & (distance_cm <= 50.0)] = 5\n   204→    out[distance_cm > 50.0] = 6\n   205→    return out\n   206→\n   207→\n   208→def discretize_position(position_cm: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n   209→    clipped = np.clip(position_cm, 0.0, TRACK_LENGTH_CM - 1e-6)\n   210→    return np.minimum((clipped / (TRACK_LENGTH_CM / 5.0)).astype(np.int64), 4)\n   211→\n   212→\n   213→def discretize_speed(speed_cm_s: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n   214→    out = np.zeros(speed_cm_s.shape, dtype=np.int64)\n   215→    out[(speed_cm_s >= 2.0) & (speed_cm_s < 10.0)] = 1\n   216→    out[(speed_cm_s >= 10.0) & (speed_cm_s < 20.0)] = 2\n   217→    out[(speed_cm_s >= 20.0) & (speed_cm_s < 40.0)] = 3\n   218→    out[speed_cm_s >= 40.0] = 4\n   219→    return out\n   220→\n   221→\n   222→def compute_distance_to_zone(position_cm: np.ndarray, zone_idx: int) -> np.ndarray:\n   223→    zone_start, zone_end = ZONE_BOUNDS[zone_idx]\n   224→    distance = np.zeros(position_cm.shape, dtype=np.float32)\n   225→    before = position_cm < zone_start\n   226→    after = position_cm > zone_end\n   227→    distance[before] = position_cm[before] - zone_start\n   228→    distance[after] = position_cm[after] - zone_end\n   229→    return distance\n   230→\n   231→\n   232→def trial_majority_zone(observed_zone: np.ndarray, start: int, stop: int, fallback: int) -> int:\n   233→    subset = observed_zone[start:stop]\n   234→    subset = subset[subset >= 0]\n   235→    if subset.size == 0:\n   236→        return fallback\n   237→    return int(np.bincount(subset).argmax())\n   238→\n   239→\n   240→def infer_trial_zones(\n   241→    positions: np.ndarray,\n   242→    reward_zone_signal: np.ndarray,\n   243→    trial_slices: list[tuple[int, int]],\n   244→    trial_rewarded: np.ndarray,\n   245→    reward_frame_idx: np.ndarray,\n   246→    trial_by_frame: np.ndarray,\n   247→    trial_env: np.ndarray,\n   248→) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n   249→    n_trials = len(trial_slices)\n   250→    observed = np.full(n_trials, -1, dtype=np.int64)\n   251→    observed_position = np.full(n_trials, np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n   252→\n   253→    reward_pos_by_trial: dict[int, float] = {}\n   254→    for frame_idx in reward_frame_idx:\n   255→ continue\n   260→        reward_pos_by_trial.setdefault(trial_id, float(positions[frame_idx]))\n   261→\n   262→    for trial_id, (start, stop_exclusive) in enumerate(trial_slices):\n   263→        mask = reward_zone_signal[start:stop_exclusive] > 0\n   264→        if np.any(mask):\n   265→            zone_pos = float(np.nanmedian(positions[start:stop_exclusive][mask]))\n   266→        elif trial_id in reward_pos_by_trial:\n   267→            zone_pos = reward_pos_by_trial[trial_id]\n   268→        else:\n   269→            zone_pos = np.nan\n   270→\n   271→        if np.isfinite(zone_pos):\n   272→            observed_position[trial_id] = zone_pos\n   273→            observed[trial_id] = int(np.argmin(np.abs(ZONE_CENTERS - zone_pos)))\n   274→\n   275→    filled = observed.copy()\n   276→    unique_observed = np.unique(observed[observed >= 0])\n   277→    unique_env = np.unique(trial_env)\n   278→\n   279→    if unique_observed.size == 0:\n   280→        raise RuntimeError(\"Could not infer any reward-zone labels from the session.\")\n   281→\n   282→    if unique_observed.size == 1:\n   283→        filled[filled < 0] = int(unique_observed[0])\n   284→        return filled, observed_position\n   285→\n   286→    if unique_observed.size > 2:\n   287→        raise RuntimeError(f\"Observed more than two reward zones within one session: {unique_observed.tolist()}\")\n   288→\n   289→    split = min(SWITCH_TRIAL_INDEX, n_trials)\n   290→    pre_majority = trial_majority_zone(observed, 0, split, fallback=int(unique_observed[0]))\n   291→    post_fallback = int(unique_observed[1] if unique_observed.size > 1 else pre_majority)\n   292→    post_majority = trial_majority_zone(observed, split, n_trials, fallback=post_fallback)\n   293→    filled[:split][filled[:split] < 0] = pre_majority\n   294→    filled[split:][filled[split:] < 0] = post_majority\n   295→\n   296→    if unique_env.size > 1 and split < n_trials:\n   297→        filled[:split] = pre_majority\n   298→        filled[split:] = post_majority\n   299→\n   300→    return filled, observed_position\n   301→\n   302→\n   303→def build_trial_slices(trial_start_signal: np.ndarray, teleport_signal: np.ndarray) -> list[tuple[int, int]]:\n   304→    starts = np.flatnonzero(trial_start_signal > 0.5)\n   305→    teleports = 416→\n   417→    fig.savefig(output_path, dpi=150)\n   418→    plt.close(fig)\n   419→\n   420→\n   421→def convert_session(\n   422→    arrays: SessionArrays,\n   423→    show_processing: bool,\n   424→) -> tuple[list[np.ndarray], list[np.ndarray], list[np.ndarray], np.ndarray, dict]:\n   425→    t0 = time.perf_counter()\n   426→\n   427→    trial_slices = build_trial_slices(arrays.trial_start, arrays.teleport)\n   428→    reward_frame_idx = reward_frames_for_session(arrays)\n   429→    rewarded_trial_ids = set(int(t) for t in arrays.trial_number[reward_frame_idx] if t >= 0)\n   430→\n   431→    trial_rewarded = np.array(\n   432→        [1 if trial_id in rewarded_trial_ids else 0 for trial_id in range(len(trial_slices))],\n   433→        dtype=np.int64,\n   434→    )\n   435→    trial_prev_rewarded = np.concatenate([[0], trial_rewarded[:-1]]).astype(np.float32, copy=False)\n   436→\n   437→    trial_env = np.zeros((len(trial_slices),), dtype=np.int64)\n   438→    lick_artifact = np.zeros((len(trial_slices),), dtype=bool)\n   439→    for trial_id, (start, stop_exclusive) in enumerate(trial_slices):\n   440→        env = arrays.environment[start:stop_exclusive]\n   441→        env = env[env >= 0]\n   442→        trial_env[trial_id] = mode_int(env, default=0)\n   443→\n   444→        lick_trial = arrays.lick[start:stop_exclusive]\n   445→        if lick_trial.size and np.mean(lick_trial > 2) > LICK_ARTIFACT_FRACTION:\n   446→            lick_artifact[trial_id] = True\n   447→\n   448→    trial_zone, observed_zone_position = infer_trial_zones(\n   449→        positions=np.clip(arrays.position, 0.0, TRACK_LENGTH_CM),\n   450→        reward_zone_signal=arrays.reward_zone_signal,\n   451→        trial_slices=trial_slices,\n   452→        trial_rewarded=trial_rewarded,\n   453→        reward_frame_idx=reward_frame_idx,\n   454→        trial_by_frame=arrays.trial_number.astype(np.int64, copy=False),\n   455→        trial_env=trial_env,\n   456→    )\n   457→\n   458→    if math.isclose(arrays.rate_hz, TARGET_RATE_HZ):\n   459→        factor = 1\n   460→    elif math.isclose(arrays.rate_hz, 2.0 * TARGET_RATE_HZ):\n   461→        factor = 2\n   462→    else:\n   463→        raise RuntimeError(f\"Unexpected sampling rate {arrays.rate_hz} in {arrays.session_label}\")\n   464→\n   465→    neural_trials: list[np.ndarray] = []\n   466→    input_trials: list[np.ndarray] = []\n   467→    output_trials: list[np.ndarray] = []\n   468→\n   469→ arrays.neural[start:stop_exclusive].T.astype(np.float32, copy=False)\n   474→        position = np.clip(arrays.position[start:stop_exclusive], 0.0, TRACK_LENGTH_CM)\n   475→        speed = np.clip(arrays.speed[start:stop_exclusive], 0.0, None)\n   476→        lick_binary = (arrays.lick[start:stop_exclusive] > 0).astype(np.int64, copy=False)\n   477→\n   478→        if factor == 2:\n   479→            neural_trial = rebin_2d_sum_time_last(neural_trial, factor)\n   480→            position = rebin_1d_mean(position, factor)\n   481→            speed = rebin_1d_mean(speed, factor)\n   482→            lick_binary = rebin_1d_any(lick_binary, factor)\n   483→        else:\n   484→            position = position.astype(np.float32, copy=False)\n   485→            speed = speed.astype(np.float32, copy=False)\n   486→            lick_binary = lick_binary.astype(np.int64, copy=False)\n   487→\n   488→        t_bins = neural_trial.shape[1]\n   489→        time_from_start = (np.arange(t_bins, dtype=np.float32) * TARGET_DT_S)\n   490→        env_series = np.full((t_bins,), float(trial_env[trial_id]), dtype=np.float32)\n   491→        trial_series = np.full((t_bins,), float(trial_id), dtype=np.float32)\n   492→        prev_reward_series = np.full((t_bins,), float(trial_prev_rewarded[trial_id]), dtype=np.float32)\n   493→\n   494→        zone_idx = int(trial_zone[trial_id])\n   495→        dist = compute_distance_to_zone(position, zone_idx)\n   496→        dist_bin = discretize_distance(dist)\n   497→        pos_bin = discretize_position(position)\n   498→        speed_bin = discretize_speed(speed)\n   499→        zone_bin = np.full((t_bins,), zone_idx, dtype=np.int64)\n   500→        reward_bin = np.full((t_bins,), int(trial_rewarded[trial_id]), dtype=np.int64)\n   501→\n   502→        input_trial = np.stack(\n   503→            [time_from_start, env_series, trial_series, prev_reward_series],\n   504→            axis=0,\n   505→        ).astype(np.float32, copy=False)\n   506→        output_trial = np.stack(\n   507→            [dist_bin, pos_bin, speed_bin, lick_binary, zone_bin, reward_bin],\n   508→            axis=0,\n   509→        ).astype(np.int64, copy=False)\n   510→\n   511→        neural_trials.append(neural_trial.astype(np.float32, copy=False))\n   512→        input_trials.append(input_trial)\n   513→        output_trials.append(output_trial)\n   514→\n   515→    if len(neural_trials) < 2:\n   516→        raise   48→### Notes\n    49→- The reference repo is for **2-photon calcium imaging**, not electrophysiology. There is no spike sorting or ephys quality filtering in this code path.\n    50→- `sess` is the base aligned session object. The README explicitly states raw `sess` objects **do not contain dF/F**; dF/F is computed later.\n    51→- `make_session_pkl.md` shows the first saved object is `sess`, created from raw suite2p + VR files after manual suite2p curation.\n    52→- Cell curation is manual in the suite2p GUI; `iscell.npy` is the curated cell mask used when loading suite2p outputs.\n    53→- `multi_anim_sess` is the main post-processing stage used by the paper:\n    54→  - loads saved `sess` pickle for each animal/day,\n    55→  - computes dF/F,\n    56→  - optionally deconvolves dF/F into `events`,\n    57→  - adds position-binned trial matrices,\n    58→  - computes place-cell statistics and trial subset metadata.\n    59→- For single-channel data, `multi_anim_sess(..., calc_spks=True)` calls `preprocessing.dff(...)` with:\n    60→  - `neuropil_method='subtract'`\n    61→  - `baseline_method='maximin'`\n    62→  - `subtract_baseline=True`\n    63→  - `neu_coef=0.7`\n    64→  - `deconvolve=True`\n    65→  - optional `keep_teleports` per animal\n    66→- `dff` restricts valid fluorescence to trial spans using `trial_start_inds` and `teleport_inds`. Details\n   186→- Task structure:\n   187→  - hidden 50 cm reward zone on a 450 cm linear VR track\n   188→  - two main environments (`ENV 1`, `ENV 2`) in the switch task\n   189→  - reward locations A/B/C are equidistant and only one is active at a time\n   190→  - reward switches occur after 30 trials on switch days\n   191→  - first 10 trials of a new condition can receive automatic reward if the mouse fails to lick in the new zone\n   192→  - teleport period follows each lap: gray jitter period plus 50 cm tunnel before the next lap\n   193→- Imaging / alignment:\n   194→  - 2-photon CA1 ROI detection\n   200→  - dF/F baseline computed independently within each trial using a `maximin` procedure with a 20 s sliding window\n   201→  - dF/F = `(F - baseline) / abs(baseline)`\n   202→  - dF/F smoothed with a two-sample Gaussian\n   203→  - deconvolved activity extracted with OASIS and used for decoder / SI analyses\n   204→- Behavioral / neural analysis conventions:\n   205→  - all cross-validation, 90% train / 10% test\n   211→  - train/test either within pre-switch trials or train pre-switch and test post-switch\n   212→  - occupancy is downsampled to match reward-relative position bins\n   213→  - shuffled controls are generated by circularly shifting each cell’s timeseries\n   214→\n   215→### Curation Steps\n   216→\n   217→**Neuron curation rules**:\n   218→- Manual suite2p curation removed ROIs:\n   219→  - with multiple somata or dendrites\n   220→  - lacking visually obvious transients\n   221→  - suspected of calcium-indicator overexpression\n   222→  - with high continuous fluorescence fluctuation typical of putative interneurons\n   223→- Additional putative interneurons were excluded if Pearson correlation between dF/F and running speed exceeded `0.5`\n   224→- Reported additional exclusion from this speed-correlation rule: `0.42 ± 0.85%` of cells across mice/days\n   225→\n   226→**Trial curation rules**:\n   227→- Reward omission trials occur randomly on ~15% of trials and are retained as a task condition\n   228→- Licking analysis removes trials with erroneous lick detection when >30% of imaging frames in the trial have cumulative lick count `> 2`\n   229→- Reported lick-artifact removal: `81 / 12,376` imaged trials across 11 switch mice (~0.65%)\n   230→- Some analyses require at least three omission trials within a trial set\n   231→\n   232→### Decoders Trained\n   233→| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n   234→| Reward-relative position from deconvolved activity | Paper reports decode score above shuffle, not the task structure constrains the active zone to one contiguous block per condition.\n   286→1. **Use omission fraction and switch timing as sanity anchors**: Raw data show `rewarded = 84.65%`, `omission = 15.35%`, matching the paper’s ~15% omission rate, and switch sessions should change condition near trial 30.\n   287→1. **Keep within-session trial number 0-indexed**: This matches raw NWB values and `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`.\n   288→\n   289→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   290→- [ ] Neural spot-check: for one single-plane session and one 31 Hz session, compare a raw NWB trial slice against converted `neural` with `np.allclose()` after ROI filtering and rebinning.\n   291→- [ ] Input spot-check: verify `time_from_trial_start`, `environment`, `trial number`, and `previous outcome` on 3 hand-checked trials loaded directly from NWB.\n   292→- [ ] Output spot-check: verify distance-to-zone bin, absolute-position bin, speed bin, and lick bin on specific raw frames loaded directly from NWB.\n   293→- [ ] Trial parsing check: confirm converted trial counts match reconstructed `trial_start` / `teleport` boundaries.\n   294→- [ ] Cohort statistics check: converted subjects/sessions/trials/neurons must match the NWB inventory and remain within paper-reported ranges.\n   295→- [ ] Outcome distribution check: rewarded/omission fraction in converted data should remain close to the raw-data fraction (`84.65% / 15.35%`).\n   296→\n   297→---\n   298→\n   299→## Step 6: Script Development\n   300→**Status**: COMPLETE"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_013qMaGNKePcAw4E4oWCvQe5","session_id":"d335c396-89d4-494d-96c6-f2a906406010","uuid":"2ba7db76-e099-4831-8ff5-97ea89c6ee24"}

DATA LOADING (from NWB files)\n\n**Source**: NWB 2.0 files in HDF5 format from DANDI 001361\n\n**Key Function**: `load_session_arrays()` (lines 319-360)\n\n**Loading Details**:\n- **Subject ID**: from `general/subject/subject_id`\n- **Session ID**: from `general/session_id`\n- **Neural data**: `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/data` - deconvolved calcium activity (already processed from dF/F + OASIS deconvolution)\n- **ROI IDs**: from `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/rois` - specifies which ROIs are in the response matrix\n- **Segmentation**: `processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation/iscell` - shape `(n_rois, 2)` where column 0 is binary cell flag\n- **Behavioral timeseries** (all aligned to imaging rate):\n  - Position: `position/data` (cm on track)\n  - Speed: `speed/data` (cm/s)\n  - Lick: `lick/data` (cumulative sensor counts)\n  - Environment: `environment/data` (0 or 1)\n  - Reward zone signal: `reward_zone/data`\n  - Scanning: `scanning/data`\n  - Trial number: `trial number/data`\n  - Trial start: `trial_start/data` (binary signal)\n  - Teleport: `teleport/data` (binary signal marking lap transitions)\n- **Reward events**: `Reward/timestamps` - sparse event times that are matched to frames\n\n**Sampling Rates**:\n- 124 sessions at `15.5078125 Hz`\n- 28 sessions at `31.015625 Hz` (m17, m18 multipane mice)\n\n---\n\n### 2. NEURON FILTERING (Cell Curation)\n\n**Logic**: Multi-step filtering applied at load time\n\n**Step 1 - ROI-Region-Aware Selection** (lines 332-336):\n```python\nneural = neural_group[\"data\"][()]  # shape (time, n_rois)\nroi_ids = neural_group[\"rois\"][()]  # ROI indices present in response matrix\niscell = seg[\"iscell\"][()][roi_ids, 0] > 0.5  # Only index using actual ROI ids\nneural = neural[:, iscell]  # Keep only curated cells\nroi_ids = roi_ids[iscell]\n```\n\n**Critical Detail**: Only apply `iscell` filtering to ROI indices referenced by the response matrix, not the full segmentation table. This avoids overcounting multipane sessions.\n\n**Result**: ~779 neurons per session on average (range 155-1780)\n\n**Notes**:\n- Manual suite2p curation removed ROIs with multiple somata, dendrites, obvious non-transient behavior, or suspected indicator overexpression\n- Paper also excluded putative interneurons with dF/F-speed correlation > 0.5, but this exact filter was not re-run on the NWB export because the reference dF/F stream was not exposed\n- Archive limitation documented\n\n---\n\n### 3. TRIAL DEFINITION (Trial Boundaries)\n\n**Function**: `build_trial_slices()` (lines 303-316)\n\n**Logic**:\n```python\nstarts = np.flatnonzero(trial_start_signal > 0.5)  # Binary edge detection\nteleports = np.flatnonzero(teleport_signal > 0.5)   # Binary edge detection\nslices = [(start, teleport + 1) for start, teleport in zip(starts[:n], teleports[:n])]\n```\n\n**Details**:\n- Trial start marked by rising edge in `trial_start` timeseries\n- Trial end marked by rising edge in `teleport` timeseries (after lap completion)\n- Each trial represented as `(start_frame_idx, stop_frame_idx_exclusive)` tuple\n- Minimum 2 trials required per session (raises RuntimeError otherwise)\n\n**Temporal Alignment**: All trials time-aligned to first frame of trial start\n\n---\n\n### 4. TRIAL QUALITY FILTERING (Lick Artifacts)\n\n**Function**: `convert_session()` (lines 438-446)\n\n**Lick Artifact Threshold**: `LICK_ARTIFACT_FRACTION = 0.35` (35%)\n\n**Logic**:\n```python\nfor trial_id, (start, stop_exclusive) in enumerate(trial_slices):\n    lick_trial = arrays.lick[start:stop_exclusive]\n    if lick_trial.size and np.mean(lick_trial > 2) > LICK_ARTIFACT_FRACTION:\n        lick_artifact[trial_id] = True  # Mark for removal\n```\n\n**Filter Rule**: If > 35% of frames in a trial have `lick > 2`, the trial is marked as containing erroneous lick sensor artifacts and excluded from the converted dataset\n\n**Result**: ~69 trials removed per full dataset (~0.56%), close to paper's reported ~0.65%\n\n**Notes**:\n- Matches reference code path in the paper's `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`\n- Based on cumulative lick count reaching threshold of 2\n- Paper reports lick-artifact removal on ~81/12,376 imaged trials\n\n---\n\n### 5. NEURAL DATA PROCESSING\n\n**Architecture**: The NWB archive provides **deconvolved** neural activity, meaning dF/F and OASIS deconvolution have already been applied\n\n**From CONVERSION_NOTES** (Steps 1 & 3):\n\nThe reference pipeline applies:\n1. **dF/F Computation**: \n   - Baseline computed within each trial using `maximin` procedure with 20s sliding window\n   - Formula: `dF/F = (F - baseline) / abs(baseline)`\n   - Smoothing applied pre-baseline and post-baseline with 2-sample Gaussian\n   \n2. **Deconvolution**: \n   - OASIS algorithm applied to dF/F to extract neural events/spikes\n   - Result stored as `events` timeseries\n\n3. **Neural Data Used**: \n   - `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/data` is the deconvolved `events` stream\n   - This is what the paper's decoder operates on (not raw fluorescence)\n\n**Shape**: `(n_timepoints, n_curated_neurons)` → transposed for decoder to `(n_curated_neurons, n_timepoints)`\n\n---\n\n### 6. BEHAVIORAL VARIABLE COMPUTATION\n\n**Position** (line 348):\n- Raw: `position/data` from NWB in cm\n- Processing: Clipped to `[0, 450]` cm track length\n- Usage: Used to compute distance-to-zone and absolute position bins\n\n**Speed** (line 349):\n- Raw: `speed/data` from NWB in cm/s\n- Processing: Clipped to `[0, None]` (non-negative)\n- Usage: Speed binning; paper excludes `< 2 cm/s` samples in some analyses\n\n**Lick** (line 350):\n- Raw: `lick/data` cumulative sensor counts\n- Processing: Binary conversion `lick > 0` per frame, then rebinned with logical OR\n- Output: `lick_binary = (lick > 0).astype(int64)`\n\n**Reward Zone Location** (lines 240-300):\n- **Function**: `infer_trial_zones()`\n- **Zone Bounds** (hardcoded):\n  - Zone A: 80-130 cm\n  - Zone B: 200-250 cm\n  - Zone C: 320-370 cm\n- **Inference Strategy**:\n  1. Use `reward_zone_signal` where it's active (> 0)\n  2. If no signal, look for reward event times within trial\n  3. Assign to nearest zone center using `argmin(|ZONE_CENTERS - position|)`\n  4. Fill omitted trials (missing labels) by majority vote within blocks before/after trial 30\n- **Output**: Integer zone index per trial (0, 1, 2)\n\n**Reward Outcome** (lines 363-367, 428-434):\n- **Function**: `reward_frames_for_session()`\n- Logic: Match `Reward/timestamps` to frame indices using `searchsorted`\n- Per-trial outcome: Trial is \"rewarded\" if any reward event falls within trial frame range\n- Output: Binary integer (0 = omitted, 1 = rewarded)\n- Result: ~15-16% omission rate (matching paper's ~15%)\n\n**Environment** (lines 437-442):\n- Per-trial modal environment value from `environment/data` \n- Binary: 0 (ENV 1) or 1 (ENV 2)\n- Rule: `trial_env[trial_id] = mode_int(env[env >= 0], default=0)`\n\n---\n\n### 7. TEMPORAL ALIGNMENT APPROACH\n\n**Alignment Point**: Trial start frame\n\n**Implementation** (lines 488-489):\n```python\nt_bins = neural_trial.shape[1]  # Number of frames after rebinning\ntime_from_start = (np.arange(t_bins, dtype=np.float32) * TARGET_DT_S)\n# TARGET_DT_S = 1.0 / 15.5078125 ≈ 0.06448 seconds per bin\n```\n\n**For Each Trial**:\n- Time 0 = first frame of `trial_start` edge\n- Time increases monotonically by `TARGET_DT_S` per frame\n- Maximum trial duration: ~20.8 seconds (320 frames at 15.5 Hz)\n\n**Frame Alignment**:\n- Neural, behavior, and inputs all sampled at common rate (15.5078125 Hz after rebinning)\n- Direct frame-to-frame correspondence maintained\n\n---\n\n### 8. REBINNING APPROACH FOR DIFFERENT SAMPLING RATES\n\n**Target Rate**: `TARGET_RATE_HZ = 15.5078125 Hz` (common across dataset)\n\n**Rebinning Factor Detection** (lines 458-463):\n```python\nif math.isclose(arrays.rate_hz, TARGET_RATE_HZ):\n    factor = 1  # No rebinning needed\nelif math.isclose(arrays.rate_hz, 2.0 * TARGET_RATE_HZ):\n    factor = 2  # 31.015625 Hz → 15.5078125 Hz\nelse:\n    raise RuntimeError(...)\n```\n\n**Rebinning Functions**:\n\n1. **Neural data** - `rebin_2d_sum_time_last()` (lines 182-193):\n   - Shape: `(n_neurons, n_time)`\n   - Method: Sum within bins (integration)\n   - Block reshape: `(n_neurons, n_full_bins, factor)` → sum axis 2\n   - Handles partial last bin\n   ```python\n   full = x[:, : n_full * factor].reshape(x.shape[0], n_full, factor).sum(axis=2)\n   ```\n\n2. **Position/Speed** - `rebin_1d_mean()` (lines 158-167):\n   - Method: Arithmetic mean within bins\n   - Block reshape: `(n_full_bins, factor)` → mean axis 1\n   ```python\n   out.append(x[: n_full * factor].reshape(n_full, factor).mean(axis=1))\n   ```\n\n3. **Lick** - `rebin_1d_any()` (lines 170-179):\n   - Method: Logical OR (any true within bin)\n   - Block reshape: `(n_full_bins, factor)` → any axis 1\n   ```python\n   out.append(np.any(x[: n_full * factor].reshape(n_full, factor) > 0, axis=1))\n   ```\n\n**Notes**:\n- Multipane sessions (m17, m18) at `31.015625 Hz` are rebinned by factor of 2\n- Handles partial last bins (remainder frames)\n- Results in uniform `15.5078125 Hz` output across all sessions\n\n---\n\n### 9. DISTANCE-TO-REWARD-ZONE COMPUTATION\n\n**Function**: `compute_distance_to_zone()` (lines 222-229)\n\n**Logic**:\n```python\nzone_start, zone_end = ZONE_BOUNDS[zone_idx]\ndistance = np.zeros(position_cm.shape, dtype=np.float32)\nbefore = position_cm < zone_start\nafter = position_cm > zone_end\ndistance[before] = position_cm[before] - zone_start   # Negative before\ndistance[after] = position_cm[after] - zone_end        # Positive after\n# Inside zone: distance = 0\n```\n\n**Output**: Signed distance in cm relative to active reward zone\n\n**Discretization** - `discretize_distance()` (lines 196-205):\n```\nBin 0: distance < -50 cm\nBin 1: -50 ≤ distance < -10\nBin 2: -10 ≤ distance < 0\nBin 3: distance == 0\nBin 4: 0 < distance ≤ 10\nBin 5: 10 < distance ≤ 50\nBin 6: distance > 50 cm\n```\n\n---\n\n### 10. POSITION BINNING\n\n**Function**: `discretize_position()` (lines 208-210)\n\n**Logic**: Divide 450 cm track into 5 equal bins\n```python\nclipped = np.clip(position_cm, 0.0, 450.0 - 1e-6)\nbins = np.minimum((clipped / 90.0).astype(int64), 4)  # 90 cm per bin\n```\n\n**Output Bins**:\n- Bin 0: 0-90 cm\n- Bin 1: 90-180 cm\n- Bin 2: 180-270 cm\n- Bin 3: 270-360 cm\n- Bin 4: 360-450 cm\n\n---\n\n### 11. SPEED BINNING\n\n**Function**: `discretize_speed()` (lines 213-219)\n\n```python\nBin 0: speed < 2 cm/s\nBin 1: 2 ≤ speed < 10\nBin 2: 10 ≤ speed < 20\nBin 3: 20 ≤ speed < 40\nBin 4: speed ≥ 40 cm/s\n```\n\n---\n\n### 12. switch task\n   189→   189→  - reward locations A/B/C are equidistant and only one is active at a time\n   190→   190→  - reward switches occur after 30 trials on switch days\n   191→   191→  - first 10 trials of a new condition can receive automatic reward if the mouse fails to lick in the new zone\n   192→   192→  - teleport period follows each lap: gray jitter period plus 50 cm tunnel before the next lap\n   193→   193→- Imaging / alignment:\n   194→   194→  - 2-photon CA1 calcium imaging with GCaMP7f\n   195→   195→  - most sessions sampled at ~15.5 Hz\n   196→   196→  - m17 and m18 were imaged in two planes interleaved at ~31 Hz, yielding ~15.5 Hz per plane\n   197→   197→  - imaging sometimes stopped during teleport periods to minimize photodamage; some animals/days were imaged through teleports\n   198→   198→- Neural preprocessing:\n   199→   199→  - Suite2P used for motion correction and ROI detection\n   200→   200→  - dF/F baseline computed independently within each trial using a `maximin` procedure with a 20 s sliding window\n   201→   201→  - dF/F = `(F - baseline) / abs(baseline)`\n   202→   202→  - dF/F smoothed with a two-sample Gaussian\n   203→   203→  - deconvolved activity extracted with OASIS and used for decoder / SI analyses\n   204→   204→- Behavioral / neural analysis conventions:\n   205→   205→  - all spatial analyses exclude neural activity when speed `< 2 cm/s`\n   206→   206→  - spatial binning is 45 bins of 10 cm each along the 450 cm track\n   207→   207→  - licking is converted to binary counts and smoothed / binned for downstream analyses\n   208→   208→- Decoder-specific paper processing:\n   209→   209→  - decoder predicts circular reward-relative position from deconvolved activity\n   210→   210→  - tenfold cross-validation, 90% train / 10% test\n   211→   211→  - train/test either within pre-switch trials or train pre-switch and test post-switch\n   212→   212→  - occupancy is downsampled to match reward-relative position bins\n   213→   213→  - shuffled controls are generated by circularly shifting each cell’s timeseries\n   214→   214→\n   215→   215→### Curation Steps\n   216→   216→\n   217→   217→**Neuron curation rules**:\n   218→   218→- Manual suite2p curation removed ROIs:\n   219→   219→  - with multiple somata or dendrites\n   220→   220→  - lacking visually obvious transients\n   221→   221→  - suspected of calcium-indicator overexpression\n   222→   222→  - with high continuous fluorescence fluctuation typical of putative interneurons\n   223→   223→- Additional putative interneurons were excluded if Pearson correlation between dF/F and running speed exceeded `0.5`\n   224→   224→- Reported additional exclusion from this speed-correlation rule: `0.42 ± 0.85%` of cells across mice/days\n   225→   225→\n   226→   226→**Trial curation rules**:\n   227→   227→- Reward omission trials occur randomly on ~15% of trials and are retained as a task condition\n   228→   228→- Licking analysis removes trials with erroneous lick detection when >30% of imaging frames in the trial have cumulative lick count `> 2`\n   229→   229→- Reported lick-artifact removal: `81 / 12,376` imaged trials across 11 switch mice (~0.65%)\n   230→   230→- Some analyses require at least three omission trials within a trial set\n   231→   231→\n   232→   232→### Decoders Trained\n   233→   233→| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n   234→   234→| Reward-relative position from deconvolved activity | Paper reports decode score above shuffle, not classification accuracy; after-switch generalization is significant only for RR cells |\n   235→   235→| Single-neuron deconvolved activity from task + movement variables (GLM) | FDE: all place cells `0.10 ± 0.19`, TR `0.32 ± 0.13`, RR `0.29 ± 0.11`, non-RR `0.29 ± 0.11` |\n   236→   236→\n   237→   237→---\n   238→   238→\n   239→   239→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   240→   240→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   241→   241→\n   242→   242→### Discrepancies Found\n   243→   243→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n   244→   244→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   245→   245→| Cohort size | Code primarily analyzes the `MetaLearn` switch cohort by day, especially the seven switch days | NWB archive contains 11 mice / 152 subject-session files | Paper reports 11 switch-task mice plus a separate 3-mouse fixed-condition cohort | Treat the shared NWB archive as the 11-mouse switch cohort only. Fixed-condition mice are not present in `data/`. |\n   246→   246→| Session count | Code expects one saved `sess` per imaging day; m11 has special handling in metadata | 152 NWB files = `11 * 14 - 2` | Paper states 14 task days, with imaging for m11 starting on day 3 | Counts are consistent once the m11 day 1-2 exception is applied. |\n   247→   247→| Neural stream to decode | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` uses `sess.timeseries['events']` after dF/F + OASIS | NWB stores `Fluorescence`, `Neuropil`, and `Deconvolved`; no explicit dF/F timeseries | Paper decoder uses deconvolved calcium activity derived after per-trial dF/F | Use NWB `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/data` as the paper-equivalent neural signal. Raw `Fluorescence`/`Neuropil` remain available for sanity checks. |\n   248→   248→| Cell filtering source | Code assumes suite2p curation has already been applied to the loaded `sess` data | NWB includes `iscell` in `PlaneSegmentation`, but segmentation tables for multipane mice contain ROIs not present in `Deconvolved/plane0/data` | Paper counts are post-curation putative pyramidal neurons | Filter cells by `iscell[:,0] > 0.5` only on ROI indices listed in `Deconvolved/plane0/rois`. This avoids overcounting plane-1 ROIs that are not in the response matrix. |\n   249→   249→| Neurons/session | Code/paper operate on curated cells after ROI QC | Initial naive data count from full segmentation exceeded paper range; corrected ROI-referenced curated count is `155–1780` per session | Paper reports `155–2172` neurons/session | After using the ROI region correctly, the data are consistent with the paper’s reported range. |\n   250→   250→| Multipane sessions | Code notes m17 and m18 were imaged in two planes and pooled for most analyses | NWB sessions for `sub-m17` and `sub-m18` have response rate `31.015625 Hz`, `planeIdx` includes `0/1`, but `Deconvolved/plane0/rois` selects only plane 0 cells | Paper states two-plane acquisition at ~31 Hz interleaved, ~15.5 Hz per plane | The archive exposes only the plane-0 response series for these files. Conversion will use the provided response matrix and document this archive-level limitation. |\n   251→   251→| Trial boundary representation | Code uses `trial_start_inds` and `teleport_inds` arrays in `sess` | NWB provides framewise binary `trial_start` and `teleport` time series plus `trial number` | Paper defines laps/trials with a teleport period after each lap | Reconstruct trial start/end indices from `trial_start` and `teleport` signals in NWB. |\n   252→   252→| Timebase | Code and methods emphasize ~15.5 Hz per plane | NWB contains both `15.5078125 Hz` and `31.015625 Hz` sessions | Paper says most sessions ~15.5 Hz and multipane mice interleaved at ~31 Hz for ~15.5 Hz/plane | Use a common output bin size during conversion so all sessions share the same decoder timebase. |\n   253→   253→| Trial totals | Data inventory gives `12,216` starts / `12,217` unique trial numbers | NWB trial counts are slightly lower than one paper count | Paper cites `12,376` imaged trials across 11 switch mice in the lick-QC section and `80.5 ± 7.4` trials/session overall | Accept NWB-exported trial identities as ground truth for conversion, while using the paper’s mean trials/session as the stronger consistency check. The remaining ~1.3% mismatch is likely a paper-vs-export counting convention difference and will be tracked in later sanity checks. |\n   254→   254→\n   255→   255→---\n   256→   256→\n   257→   257→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   258→   258→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   259→   259→\n   260→   260→### Variable Mapping\n   261→   261→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   262→   262→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   263→   263→| `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/data` | `neural` | Crop to valid frame range, select ROI indices from `Deconvolved/plane0/rois`, keep curated cells by `iscell[:,0] > 0.5`, optionally exclude speed-correlated interneuron-like cells, transpose to `(neurons, time)`, split by trials, and rebin `31.015625 Hz` sessions to the common `15.5078125 Hz` bin size | `preprocessing.dff`, `utilities.multi_anim_sess`, `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` | Neural decoder signal should match paper deconvolved activity (`events`) as closely as possible. |\n   264→   264→| `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/rois` + `processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation/iscell` | `neural` / `brain_region_idx` | Use ROI region referenced by response matrix, then `iscell[:,0] > 0.5` to keep curated cells only | suite2p curation assumption in `make_session_pkl.md`; `utilities.multi_anim_sess` | Critical for multipane sessions; do not use the full segmentation table. |\n   265→   265→| `general/optophysiology/ImagingPlane/location` | `brain_regions` | Normalize `hippocampus, CA1` to `CA1` | N/A | All sessions are CA1 in this archive. |\n   266→   266→| Reconstructed trial-relative elapsed time | `input[0]` | Seconds since trial start for each bin | trial handling implicit in `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` | Align all trials to the first frame of the trial. |\n   267→   267→| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/environment/data` | `input[1]` | Per trial, take modal valid environment value (`0` or `1`) and repeat across bins | session/task logic in `behavior.get_trial_types` / paper task description | Map `0 -> ENV1`, `1 -> ENV2`. |\n   268→   268→| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/trial number/data` | `input[2]` | Per trial, use within-session trial index and repeat across bins | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` creates 0-indexed `trial_ids` | Keep native 0-indexing to match code conventions. |\n   269→   269→| Trial reward outcome shifted by one trial | `input[3]` | Previous trial rewarded (`1`) vs omitted (`0`), repeated across current-trial bins; first trial gets `0` | paper reward omission schedule; reward outcome from trial parsing | User-requested decoder input, not a paper decoder variable. |\n   270→   270→| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/position/data` + inferred reward-zone bounds | `output[0]` | Signed distance to nearest point in active reward zone: negative before zone, `0` inside zone, positive after zone; discretize to 7 bins per task spec | reward-zone logic from `behavior.get_reward_zones`; reward-relative computations in `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` | For zone `[start, end]`: `pos < start -> pos-start`; `start <= pos <= end -> 0`; `pos > end -> pos-end`. |\n   271→   271→| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/position/data` | `output[1]` | Discretize absolute position on the 450 cm corridor into 5 equal bins | paper: 45 spatial bins over 450 cm | Decoder spec requires 5 bins, not the paper’s 45 bins. |\n   272→   272→| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/speed/data` | `output[2]` | Discretize speed into bins: `<2`, `2-10`, `10-20`, `20-40`, `>40 cm/s` | paper and code use speed threshold 2 cm/s | Keep all bins for output even though paper excludes `<2 cm/s` in some analyses. |\n   273→   273→| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/lick/data` | `output[3]` | Convert to binary per bin (`lick > 0`) after lick-artifact handling | lick cleanup in `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`; methods licking QC | For rebinned 31 Hz sessions, use logical OR within each 15.5 Hz bin. |\n   274→   274→| Inferred active reward zone per trial | `output[4]` | Map trial reward location to categorical `{A:0, B:1, C:2}` and repeat across trial bins | `behavior.get_reward_zones`; paper task design | Infer from reward-zone-active positions / reward events and fill omission trials within stable blocks. |\n   275→   275→| Reward events assigned to trials | `output[5]` | Trial outcome categorical `{omitted:0, rewarded:1}` repeated across trial bins | `behavior.get_trial_types` | Rewarded if any `Reward` event timestamp falls within the trial. |\n   276→   276→\n   277→   277→### Key Decisions\n   278→   278→1. **Use all 152 NWB sessions from the 11 switch-task mice**: The shared archive corresponds to the main switch cohort, not the 3 fixed-condition mice.\n   279→   279→1. **Use NWB `Deconvolved` as the neural signal**: The paper’s decoder and SI analyses operate on deconvolved activity after dF/F, and this stream is already provided in the archive.\n   280→   280→1. **Filter cells with ROI-region-aware `iscell` selection**: Only ROI ids referenced by the response matrix are valid candidates for inclusion.\n   281→   281→1. **Plan to exclude putative interneuron-like cells**: The paper excludes cells with dF/F-speed correlation `> 0.5`, so the conversion script should implement this if feasible from the NWB fluorescence streams and reference `dff` logic.\n   282→   282→1. **Reconstruct trials from `trial_start` and `teleport`**: This matches the reference trial definition more closely than relying only on trial-number changes.\n   283→   283→1. **Standardize all sessions to a common `15.5078125 Hz` bin size**: This is the dominant dataset rate and the paper’s effective per-plane sampling rate.\n   284→   284→1. **Use time-varying outputs for all decoder targets**: Even per-trial outputs (`reward zone`, `reward outcome`) will be repeated across bins for consistent `(n_output, n_timepoints)` arrays.\n   285→   285→1. **Infer reward-zone location from rewarded trials and fill omission trials within stable blocks**: Missing zone labels in the NWB export are concentrated on omission trials; the task structure constrains the active zone to one contiguous block per condition.\n   286→   286→1. **Use omission fraction and switch timing as sanity anchors**: Raw data show `rewarded = 84.65%`, `omission = 15.35%`, matching the paper’s ~15% omission rate, and switch sessions should change condition near trial 30.\n   287→   287→1. **Keep within-session trial number 0-indexed**: This matches raw NWB values and `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`.\n   288→   288→\n   289→   289→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   290→   290→- [ ] Neural spot-check: for one single-plane session and one 31 Hz session, compare a raw NWB trial slice against converted `neural` with `np.allclose()` after ROI filtering and rebinning.\n   291→   291→- [ ] Input spot-check: verify `time_from_trial_start`, `environment`, `trial number`, and `previous outcome` on 3 hand-checked trials loaded directly from NWB.\n   292→   292→- [ ] Output spot-check: verify distance-to-zone bin, absolute-position bin, speed bin, and lick bin on specific raw frames loaded directly from NWB.\n   293→   293→- [ ] Trial parsing check: confirm converted trial counts match reconstructed `trial_start` / `teleport` boundaries.\n   294→   294→- [ ] Cohort statistics check: converted subjects/sessions/trials/neurons must match the NWB inventory and remain within paper-reported ranges.\n   295→   295→- [ ] Outcome distribution check: rewarded/omission fraction in converted data should remain close to the raw-data fraction (`84.65% / 15.35%`).\n   296→   296→\n 147→\n   148→   148→def select_session_files(files: list[str], sample_mode: bool) -> list[str]:\n   149→   149→    if not sample_mode:\n   150→   150→        return files\n   151→   151→    chosen = chosen\n   156→   156→\n   157→   157→\n   158→   158→def rebin_1d_mean(x: np.ndarray, factor: int) -> np.ndarray:\n   159→   159→    if factor == 1:\n   160→   160→        return x.astype(np.float32, copy=False)\n   161→   161→    n_full = x.shape[0] // factor\n   162→   162→    out = []\n   163→   163→    if n_full:\n   164→   164→        out.append(x[: n_full * factor].reshape(n_full, factor).mean(axis=1))\n   165→   165→    if x.shape[0] % factor:\n   166→   166→        out.append(np.array([x[n_full * factor :].mean()], dtype=np.float32))\n   167→   167→    return np.concatenate(out).astype(np.float32, copy=False)\n   168→   168→\n   169→   169→\n   170→   170→def rebin_1d_any(x: np.ndarray, factor: int) -> np.ndarray:\n   171→   171→    if factor == 1:\n   172→   172→        return x.astype(np.int64, copy=False)\n   173→   173→    n_full = x.shape[0] // factor\n   174→   174→    out = []\n   175→   175→    if n_full:\n   176→   176→        out.append(np.any(x[: n_full * factor].reshape(n_full, factor) > 0, axis=1))\n   177→   177→    if x.shape[0] % factor:\n   178→   178→        out.append(np.array([np.any(x[n_full * factor :] > 0)], dtype=bool))\n   179→   179→    return np.concatenate(out).astype(np.int64, copy=False)\n   180→   180→\n   181→   181→\n   182→   182→def rebin_2d_sum_time_last(x: np.ndarray, factor: int) -> np.ndarray:\n   183→   183→    if factor == 1:\n   184→   184→        return x.astype(np.float32, copy=False)\n   185→   185→    t = x.shape[1]\n   186→   186→    n_full = t // factor\n   187→   187→    parts = []\n   188→   188→    if n_full:\n   189→   189→        full = x[:, : n_full * factor].reshape(x.shape[0], n_full, factor).sum(axis=2)\n   190→   190→        parts.append(full)\n   191→   191→    if t % factor:\n   192→   192→        parts.append(x[:, n_full * factor :].sum(axis=1, keepdims=True))\n   193→   193→    return np.concatenate(parts, axis=1).astype(np.float32, copy=False)\n   194→   194→\n   195→   195→\n   196→   196→def discretize_distance(distance_cm: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n   197→   197→    out = np.zeros(distance_cm.shape, dtype=np.int64)\n   198→   198→    out[distance_cm < -50.0] = 0\n   199→   199→    out[(distance_cm >= -50.0) & (distance_cm < -10.0)] = 1\n   200→   200→    out[(distance_cm >= -10.0) & (distance_cm < 0.0)] = 2\n   201→   201→    out[distance_cm == 0.0] = 3\n   202→   202→    out[(distance_cm > 0.0) & (distance_cm <= 10.0)] = 4\n   203→   203→    out[(distance_cm > 10.0) & (distance_cm <= 50.0)] = 5\n   204→   204→    out[distance_cm > 50.0] = 6\n   205→   205→    return out\n   206→   206→\n   207→   207→\n   208→   208→def discretize_position(position_cm: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n   209→   209→    clipped = np.clip(position_cm, 0.0, TRACK_LENGTH_CM - 1e-6)\n   210→   210→    return np.minimum((clipped / (TRACK_LENGTH_CM / 5.0)).astype(np.int64), 4)\n   211→   211→\n   212→   212→\n   213→   213→def discretize_speed(speed_cm_s: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n   214→   214→    out = np.zeros(speed_cm_s.shape, dtype=np.int64)\n   215→   215→    out[(speed_cm_s >= 2.0) & (speed_cm_s < 10.0)] = 1\n   216→   216→    out[(speed_cm_s >= 10.0) & (speed_cm_s < 20.0)] = 2\n   217→   217→    out[(speed_cm_s >= 20.0) & (speed_cm_s < 40.0)] = 3\n   218→   218→    out[speed_cm_s >= 40.0] = 4\n   219→   219→    return out\n   220→   220→\n   221→   221→\n   222→   222→def compute_distance_to_zone(position_cm: np.ndarray, zone_idx: int) -> np.ndarray:\n   223→   223→    zone_start, zone_end = ZONE_BOUNDS[zone_idx]\n   224→   224→    distance = np.zeros(position_cm.shape, dtype=np.float32)\n   225→   225→    before = position_cm < zone_start\n   226→   226→    after = position_cm > zone_end\n   227→   227→    distance[before] = position_cm[before] - zone_start\n   228→   228→    distance[after] = position_cm[after] - zone_end\n   229→   229→    return distance\n   230→   230→\n   231→   231→\n   232→   232→def trial_majority_zone(observed_zone: np.ndarray, start: int, stop: int, fallback: int) -> int:\n   233→   233→    subset = observed_zone[start:stop]\n   234→   234→    subset = subset[subset >= 0]\n   235→   235→    if subset.size == 0:\n   236→   236→        return fallback\n   237→   237→    return int(np.bincount(subset).argmax())\n   238→   238→\n   239→   239→\n   240→   240→def infer_trial_zones(\n   241→   241→    positions: np.ndarray,\n   242→   242→    reward_zone_signal: np.ndarray,\n   243→   243→    trial_slices: list[tuple[int, int]],\n   244→   244→    trial_rewarded: np.ndarray,\n   245→   245→    reward_frame_idx: np.ndarray,\n   246→   246→    trial_by_frame: np.ndarray,\n   247→   247→    trial_env: np.ndarray,\n   248→   248→) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n   249→   249→    n_trials = len(trial_slices)\n   250→   250→    observed = np.full(n_trials, -1, dtype=np.int64)\n   251→   251→    observed_position = np.full(n_trials, np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n   252→   252→\n   253→   253→    reward_pos_by_trial: dict[int, float] = {}\n   254→   254→    for frame_idx in reward_frame_idx:\n   255→   255→   259→   259→            continue\n   260→   260→        reward_pos_by_trial.setdefault(trial_id, float(positions[frame_idx]))\n   261→   261→\n   262→   262→    for trial_id, (start, stop_exclusive) in enumerate(trial_slices):\n   263→   263→        mask = reward_zone_signal[start:stop_exclusive] > 0\n   264→   264→        if np.any(mask):\n   265→   265→            zone_pos = float(np.nanmedian(positions[start:stop_exclusive][mask]))\n   266→   266→        elif trial_id in reward_pos_by_trial:\n   267→   267→            zone_pos = reward_pos_by_trial[trial_id]\n   268→   268→        else:\n   269→   269→            zone_pos = np.nan\n   270→   270→\n   271→   271→        if np.isfinite(zone_pos):\n   272→   272→            observed_position[trial_id] = zone_pos\n   273→   273→            observed[trial_id] = int(np.argmin(np.abs(ZONE_CENTERS - zone_pos)))\n   274→   274→\n   275→   275→    filled = observed.copy()\n   276→   276→    unique_observed = np.unique(observed[observed >= 0])\n   277→   277→    unique_env = np.unique(trial_env)\n   278→   278→\n   279→   279→    if unique_observed.size == 0:\n   280→   280→        raise RuntimeError(\"Could not infer any reward-zone labels from the session.\")\n   281→   281→\n   282→   282→    if unique_observed.size == 1:\n   283→   283→        filled[filled < 0] = int(unique_observed[0])\n   284→   284→        return filled, observed_position\n   285→   285→\n   286→   286→    if unique_observed.size > 2:\n   287→   287→        raise RuntimeError(f\"Observed more than two reward zones within one session: {unique_observed.tolist()}\")\n   288→   288→\n   289→   289→    split = min(SWITCH_TRIAL_INDEX, n_trials)\n   290→   290→    pre_majority = trial_majority_zone(observed, 0, split, fallback=int(unique_observed[0]))\n   291→   291→    post_fallback = int(unique_observed[1] if unique_observed.size > 1 else pre_majority)\n   292→   292→    post_majority = trial_majority_zone(observed, split, n_trials, fallback=post_fallback)\n   293→   293→    filled[:split][filled[:split] < 0] = pre_majority\n   294→   294→    filled[split:][filled[split:] < 0] = post_majority\n   295→   295→\n   296→   296→    if unique_env.size > 1 and split < n_trials:\n   297→   297→        filled[:split] = pre_majority\n   298→   298→        filled[split:] = post_majority\n   299→   299→\n   300→   300→    return filled, observed_position\n   301→   301→\n   302→   302→\n   303→   303→def build_trial_slices(trial_start_signal: 421→   421→def convert_session(\n   422→   422→    arrays: SessionArrays,\n   423→   423→    show_processing: bool,\n   424→   424→) -> tuple[list[np.ndarray], list[np.ndarray], list[np.ndarray], How does the reference code load and manipulate this data?\n   332→   332→  - When possible, import or copy code from the reference code in `code` directory. \n   333→   333→- Consult CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4 and reference materials to determine:\n   334→   334→  - Temporal binning for spike rates (if applicable)\n   335→   335→  - Temporal alignment of trials\n   336→   336→  - Data filtering and curation rules for neurons, trials, and sessions\n   337→   337→  - Brain regions recorded from\n   338→   338→- Mapping should be consistent with CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4.\n   339→   339→- Check for variables indicating valid data periods — exclude invalid data\n   340→   340→- Note all decisions, edge cases, and data quality issues\n   341→   341→- Plan and document **sanity and consistency checks** based on dataset size information from the reference texts, code, and data\n   342→   342→\n   343→   343→**Done when**:\n   344→   344→- You have a complete mapping document in CONVERSION_NOTES.md specifying every source-to-target variable mapping under \"Step 5\"\n   345→   345→- You have documented all decisions under \"Step 5\"\n   346→   346→- You have documented sanity and consistency checks under \"Step 5\"\n   347→   347→\n   348→   348→**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 6, update Step 5 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   349→   349→\n   350→   350→---\n   351→   351→\n   352→   352→### Step 6: Script Development\n   353→   353→\n   354→   354→**Goal**: Write the conversion script to `convert_data.py`.\n   355→   355→\n   356→   356→**Actions**:\n   357→   357→- Write all conversion code in the file `convert_data.py`\n   358→   358→- Script should run as **`python -u convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`**\n   359→   359→- Include options:\n   360→   360→  - `--full` (default): Process all sessions\n   361→   361→  - `--sample`: Process only 2 sessions for testing\n   362→   362→  - `--show-processing`: Plot visualizations of EVERY processing step for up to 2 sessions. Save plots as `processing_<session_id>.png`.\n   363→   363→- Reuse code from reference code bases where appropriate\n   364→   364→- Write efficient code:\n   365→   365→  - Vectorize loops\n   366→   366→  - Avoid unnecessary file I/O\n   367→   367→  - Use parallel processing if beneficial\n   368→   368→  - Print timing information to find bottlenecks\n   369→   369→- Plots for `--show-processing` mode should visually convince the user that every step of the conversion is correct.\n   370→   370→  - There are no temporal misalignments\n   371→   371→  - Discretization of continuous outputs is correct\n   372→   372→- Write clear, well-documented functions\n   373→   373→- Handle missing data appropriately (consult references, use sensible defaults, document)\n   374→   374→- Validate data shapes and types at each step\n   375→   375→- Include sanity checks (e.g., trial counts match across arrays)\n   376→   376→\n   377→   377→**Done when**: `convert_data.py` exists and runs efficiently without errors.\n   378→   378→\n   379→   379→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 7 until `convert_data.py` exists and you have updated Step 6 in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   380→   380→\n   381→   381→---\n   382→   382→\n   383→   383→### Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   384→   384→\n   385→   385→**Goal**: Validate the conversion on a small sample before processing the full dataset.\n   386→   386→\n   387→   387→**Actions**:\n   388→   388→1. Run `python -u convert_data.py sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing 2>&1 | tee conversion_sample_out.txt`\n   389→   389→  a. Verify the sample data structure matches the specification.\n   390→   390→  b. Check dimensions are consistent across trials/sessions.\n   391→   391→  c. Check that no data is missed during conversion. \n   392→   392→  d. Validate that metadata accurately describes the data.\n   393→   393→2. Improve efficiency of conversion.\n   394→   394→  a. Look at and note timing information for the conversion. \n   395→   395→  b. Estimate how long the full conversion will run. When you do this, make sure that your estimate accounts for differences in lengths/numbers of trials/sessions. \n   396→   396→  c. If full conversion time estimate is longer than 15 minutes, speed up the code by writing more efficient code (vectorizing loops, removing unnecessary or redundant computations) and/or including parallel processing. \n   397→   397→3. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_sample_out.txt`.\n   398→   398→4. Analyze the output file `verification_sample_out.txt`:\n   399→   399→  a. Verify no errors reported.\n   400→   400→  b. Attempt to address any warnings\n   401→   401→  c. Check input ranges and output value distributions against expectations from reference texts.\n   402→   402→\n   403→   403→**Done when**: \n   404→   404→- `sample_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n   405→   405→- `verification_sample_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n   406→   406→- Processing plots show no anomalies\n   407→   407→- Document sample statistics in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 7\"\n   408→   408→\n   409→   409→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 8 until `sample_data.pkl` and `verification_sample_out.txt` files exist, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 7 is filled in. \n   410→   410→\n   411→   411→---\n   412→   412→\n   413→   413→### Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n   414→   414→\n   415→   415→**Goal**: Verify the decoder runs successfully on sample data.\n   416→   416→\n   417→   417→**Actions**:\n   418→   418→1. Review your time estimate from Step 7.\n   441→   441→2. If estimated time is long (e.g. longer than 15 minutes), optimize bottlenecks first.\n   442→   442→3. Run `python -u convert_data.py converted_data.pkl --full 2>&1 | tee conversion_full_out.txt`\n   443→   443→4. As the code runs, update your estimates of how long processing will take. If it is much longer than your previous estimate (> 1.5x), kill the process, optimize bottlenecks, and repeat. \n   444→   444→5. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_full_out.txt`. \n   445→   445→6. Check that no data was lost during conversion \n   446→   446→7. Spot-check a few sessions to verify data integrity\n   447→   447→8. **Check for consistency** between dataset statistics in `verification_full_out.txt` and the reference texts\n   448→   448→9. Investigate any inconsistencies, and revise the conversion script until all dataset statistics are consistent\n   449→   449→\n   450→   450→**Done when**:\n   451→   451→- `converted_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n   452→   452→- `verification_full_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n   453→   453→- **ALL** dataset statistics are consistent with values from reference texts\n   454→   454→- You have documented statistics and consistency in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 9\"\n   455→   455→\n   456→   456→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 10 until `converted_data.pkl` and `verification_full_out.txt` files exist and CONVERSION_NOTES.MD Step 9 is filled in.\n   457→   457→\n   458→   458→---\n   459→   459→\n   460→   460→### Step 10: Critical Review 1\n   461→   461→\n   462→   462→**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain.\n   463→   463→\n   464→   464→**Check 1: Output log verification**\n   465→   465→Read through `verification_full_out.txt` to find and fix **all errors**. Address each warning by either fixing its cause or verifying it that it is not possible to fix it. Explain why each warning could not be fixed in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   466→   466→\n   467→   467→**Check 2: Construct sanity checks**\n   468→   468→- Develop at least one sanity check each on neural, input, and output data.\n   469→   469→- These must involve loading in the original data files (NOT through your conversion code).\n   470→   470→- Construct comparison tests and criteria using `np.allclose()`. \n   471→   471→- These tests can be spot-checks of the data, e.g. neural activity at trial 5, timepoint 10, neuron 3. \n   472→   472→- Fix all mismatches/sanity check failures. \n   473→   473→- Document your sanity checks to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   474→   474→\n   475→   475→**Check 3: Reference code comparison**\n   476→   476→- Compare your code to the reference code and methods. \n   477→   477→- For each major processing step — (a) data loading, (b) neuron/trial filtering, (c) temporal alignment, (d) binning, (e) input construction, (f) output construction — identify the corresponding code in your script and in the reference, and compare the logic. \n   478→   478→- Check whether you use the same variables, filtering, and processing as the reference code. \n   479→   479→- Fix any mismatches. \n   480→   480→- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   481→   481→- Explain your reasoning for any differences in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`.\n   482→   482→\n   483→   483→**Check 4: Key statistics comparison**\n   484→   484→- Verify that key statistics (number of animals, sessions, trials, neurons, input and output ranges, output distributions) match information available in the reference paper. \n   485→   485→- Write code to investigate any discrepancies. \n   486→   486→- Fix any discovered issues. \n   487→   487→- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   488→   488→\n   489→   489→**Check 5: Check for edge cases**\n   490→   490→- Check for off-by-one errors at the beginning/ends of trials, sessions, and subjects, or in definitions of the inputs and outputs. \n   491→   491→- There may be minor issues in the data that your code must handle. Check that your code robustly handles issues in the original data. \n   492→   492→\n   493→   493→**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n   494→   494→1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n   495→   495→2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n   496→   496→3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n   497→   497→4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n   498→   498→\n   499→   499→Write a report to CONVERSION_NOTES.md describing **every** check you did, to help convince the user that the conversion code works. **Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 10\".\n   500→   500→\n   501→   501→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 11 until you have checked for consistency between **every** statistic in the reference texts, code, and data and documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Step 10. \n   502→   502→\n   503→   503→---\n   504→   504→\n   505→   505→### Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n   506→   506→\n   507→   507→**Goal**: Validate the decoder on the **complete** dataset.\n   508→   508→\n   509→   509→**⚠️ IMPORTANT**: This step must complete fully. Do not skip or abbreviate. Do not proceed to Step 12 until this step is completely finished.\n   510→   510→\n   511→   511→**Actions**:\n   512→   512→1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --plot-samples 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_full_out.txt`. If the GPU does not have sufficient RAM for the network training, use the flag `--cpu` to specify to use the CPU to train. \n   513→   513→2. **Wait for complete execution** — this may take significant time for large datasets\n   514→   514→3. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n   515→   515→4. **Check accuracy**: High accuracy is an indicator that data conversion has been done correctly. Accuracy near chance is a sign that there might be issues in temporal alignment of signals, choice of data streams, or filtering or processing of data. Compare accuracy to expectations based on the paper. \n   516→   516→\n   517→   517→**Done when**: \n   518→   518→1. Full decoder training completes (the script finishes running)\n   519→   519→2. Accuracy results are documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 11\" with a table of accuracies\n   520→   520→\n   521→   521→---\n   522→   522→\n   523→   523→### Step 12: Critical Review 2\n   524→   524→\n   525→   525→**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain. Perform all of the following checks:\n   526→   526→\n   527→   527→**Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis**\n   528→   528→- For each output, compare validation accuracy to chance (1/num_classes)\n   529→   529→- Accuracy below chance indicates a bug, investigate and fix conversion issues to improve this. \n   530→   530→- Accuracy below 1.5x chance may also indicate a bug, investigate whether changes in conversion logic could improve accuracy. \n   531→   531→\n   532→   532→**Check 2: Accuracy comparison to paper**\n   533→   533→- Find every decoding accuracy reported in the reference paper\n   534→   534→- Create a table comparing your accuracy to the paper's accuracy for each variable\n   535→   535→- If your accuracy is lower than the reported accuracy, this may indicate a bug in your conversion that you must fix. \n   536→   536→- Differences in algorithm/architecture should be relatively small because of the data dimensionality and size. Do not use this as an excuse to dismiss differences, investigate them completely before moving on. \n   537→   537→\n   538→   538→**Check 3: Train vs validation gap**\n   539→   539→- If training accuracy is much higher (>1.5x) than validation accuracy for any output, investigate overfitting or data leakage. \n   540→   540→\n   541→   541→**If accuracy is low for an output, try these specific debugging steps:**\n   542→   542→1. Verify the output values are correct by loading raw data and checking 3 specific trials\n   543→   543→2. Check temporal alignment — plot neural + output for a single trial to verify they're synchronized\n   544→   544→3. Check whether the output has enough variation (not 99% one class)\n   545→   545→4. Check that filtering steps for neural activity streams are followed. \n   546→   546→5. Check that processing of the data matches the reference code and paper. \n   547→   547→\n   548→   548→**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n   549→   549→1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n   550→   550→2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n   551→   551→3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n   552→   552→4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n   553→   553→\n   554→   554→**Report all checks and results** to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` to convince the user that the conversion and decoding has been done correctly. \n   555→   555→\n   556→   556→**Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 12\".\n   557→   557→\n   558→   558→---\n   559→   559→\n   560→   560→### Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\n   561→   561→\n   562→   562→**Goal**: Finalize documentation and organize files.\n   563→   563→\n   564→   564→**Actions**:\n   565→   565→- Ensure CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete with all:\n   566→   566→  - Key decisions and rationale\n   567→   567→  - Checks for correctness of the conversion\n   568→   568→  - Validation results and tables\n   569→   569→  - Any issues found and how they were resolved\n   570→   570→- Create a user-facing `README.md` summarizing:\n   571→   571→  - Dataset description\n   572→   572→  - How to load and use the converted data\n   573→   573→  - Output format specification\n   574→   574→  - Key statistics\n   575→   575→- Move analysis/investigation scripts to a `cache/` folder\n   576→   576→- Create `cache/README_CACHE.md` documenting cached files\n   577→   577→\n   578→   578→**Done when**: README.md exists, CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete, and directory is clean.\n   579→   579→\n   580→   580→---\n   581→   581→\n   582→   582→## CONVERSION_NOTES.md Template\n   583→   583→\n   584→   584→**Create this file IMMEDIATELY in Step 0. How are the data split into trials?\n\ni. Trials are reconstructed from the binary `trial_start` and `teleport` signals within each session. Rising edges in `trial_start` mark trial onsets, and rising edges in `teleport` mark trial ends (inclusive). Each trial is a contiguous slice `(start_frame, stop_frame_exclusive)`.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef build_trial_slices(trial_start_signal: np.ndarray, teleport_signal: np.ndarray) -> list[tuple[int, int]]:\n    starts = np.flatnonzero(trial_start_signal > 0.5)\n    teleports = np.flatnonzero(teleport_signal > 0.5)\n    n = min(starts.size, teleports.size)\n    slices: list[tuple[int, int]] = []\n    for start, stop in zip(starts[:n], teleports[:n]):\n        if stop < start:\n            continue\n        slices.append((int(start), int(stop) + 1))\n    if len(slices) < 2:\n        raise RuntimeError(\"Need at least two trials in a session.\")\n    return slices\n```\n\niii. The AI justified this approach by referencing the paper's use of `trial_start_inds` and `teleport_inds` for trial boundaries. The `+1` on the teleport index makes the slice exclusive, capturing the full trial including the teleport frame.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Trials are filtered based on lick sensor artifacts. If more than 35% of frames in a trial have a lick value > 2 (indicating erroneous cumulative lick counts), the trial is excluded. This is the only trial-level quality filter applied.\n\nii.\n```python\nLICK_ARTIFACT_FRACTION = 0.35\n\nfor trial_id, (start, stop_exclusive) in enumerate(trial_slices):\n    lick_trial = arrays.lick[start:stop_exclusive]\n    if lick_trial.size and np.mean(lick_trial > 2) > LICK_ARTIFACT_FRACTION:\n        lick_artifact[trial_id] = True\n\n# Later in the trial loop:\nif lick_artifact[trial_id]:\n    continue\n```\n\niii. The AI referenced `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` in the reference code, which suppresses lick sensor artifacts when cumulative licks are implausibly high. The threshold of 0.35 (35%) is stated to match the reference code. The paper reports 81/12,376 trials removed (~0.65%); the AI's code removed 69/12,216 (~0.56%), close to the paper value.\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. Neural data is derived from `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/data` in each NWB file, which contains the deconvolved calcium activity (events) after dF/F computation and OASIS deconvolution. The ROI indices come from `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/rois`.\n\nii.\n```python\nneural = neural_group[\"data\"][()].astype(np.float32, copy=False)\nroi_ids = neural_group[\"rois\"][()].astype(np.int64, copy=False)\n```\n\niii. The AI justified using `Deconvolved` rather than raw `Fluorescence` because the paper's decoder and SI analyses operate on deconvolved activity (`events`). The NWB archive already provides the deconvolved stream, so re-computing dF/F from raw fluorescence was unnecessary.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. The neural data is: (1) filtered to keep only curated cells via `iscell`, (2) truncated to match behavioral frame count, (3) transposed from (time, neurons) to (neurons, time), (4) sliced per-trial, and (5) rebinned from 31 Hz to 15.5 Hz for multipane sessions by summing pairs of consecutive frames.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Cell filtering at load time:\niscell = seg[\"iscell\"][()][roi_ids, 0] > 0.5\nneural = neural[:, iscell]\n\n# Per-trial extraction and rebinning:\nneural_trial = arrays.neural[start:stop_exclusive].T.astype(np.float32, copy=False)\nif factor == 2:\n    neural_trial = rebin_2d_sum_time_last(neural_trial, factor)\n\ndef rebin_2d_sum_time_last(x: np.ndarray, factor: int) -> np.ndarray:\n    # Sum within bins for integration\n    full = x[:, : n_full * factor].reshape(x.shape[0], n_full, factor).sum(axis=2)\n```\n\niii. The AI documented that deconvolved activity is the paper-equivalent neural signal and that rebinning uses summation (integration) to preserve total event counts when downsampling from 31 Hz to 15.5 Hz.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Neurons are filtered using the suite2p `iscell` flag, applied only to ROI indices referenced by the response matrix (`Deconvolved/plane0/rois`). Only cells with `iscell[:, 0] > 0.5` are kept. The paper's additional putative interneuron filter (dF/F-speed correlation > 0.5) was NOT implemented.\n\nii.\n```python\nroi_ids = neural_group[\"rois\"][()].astype(np.int64, copy=False)\niscell = seg[\"iscell\"][()][roi_ids, 0] > 0.5\nneural = neural[:, iscell]\nroi_ids = roi_ids[iscell]\n```\n\niii. The zone bounds match the paper: \"zone A, 80-130 cm; zone B, 200-250 cm; zone C, 320-370 cm\".\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Continuous distance is discretized into 7 bins following the instructions exactly.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_distance(distance_cm: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    out = np.zeros(distance_cm.shape, dtype=np.int64)\n    out[distance_cm < -50.0] = 0\n    out[(distance_cm >= -50.0) & (distance_cm < -10.0)] = 1\n    out[(distance_cm >= -10.0) & (distance_cm < 0.0)] = 2\n    out[distance_cm == 0.0] = 3\n    out[(distance_cm > 0.0) & (distance_cm <= 10.0)] = 4\n    out[(distance_cm > 10.0) & (distance_cm <= 50.0)] = 5\n    out[distance_cm > 50.0] = 6\n    return out\n```\n\niii.
